\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{\textup{FZ}}
and the 2-form field of
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{\textup{R}}
, together with an additional Majorana fermion and a real scalar, yielding a total of sixteen bosonic and sixteen fermionic degrees of freedom.

With respect to off-shell supergravity, this multiplet
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
defines what are known as non-minimal off-shell formulations
[
140
]
.

2.4
The coupling to supergravity of the massive spin-2 field

The off-shell formulations of supergravity and the supercurrent superfield formalism introduced in Section
2.3
set the stage for presenting our findings in
[
4
]
. As we remarked at the beginning of this chapter when discussing in Section
2.1
the explicit construction of supergravity through the Noether procedure, and then further emphasized in Section
2.3
, the supercurrent superfield encodes, by construction, the leading-order coupling to pure supergravity of the given set of fields under consideration. It contains in fact the current of supersymmetry and the stress-energy tensor, which are the currents of the local Super-Poincaré algebra, and define therefore the minimal coupling to the gravitino and the metric, as in eq. (
2.1.3
) and (
2.1.5
). Therefore, constructing explicitly the supercurrent superfield associated with a supersymmetric multiplet allows one to probe directly its coupling to supergravity at leading order in the Planck mass
M_{\scalebox{0.6}{P}}
, without relying on a specific lagrangian (see for example
[
145
,
146
]
). We take this perspective in
[
4
]
and use the formalism of the supercurrent superfield to investigate the coupling to (off-shell) supergravity of the massive spin-2 field.

Constructing reliable effective theories of a single massive spin-2 field coupled to gravity is well known to be a difficult task. First of all, being a massive field of higher-spin, the massive spin-2 theories are likely to propagate ghosts. At the quadratic order, the only ghost-free theory in flat space for massive spin-2 fields is the famous Fierz--Pauli lagrangian
20
20

20

In eq. (
2.4.1
), the symbol
h_{\mu\nu}
denotes the massive spin-2 field, of mass
m
, and we employed the standard definition
h\equiv h^{\mu}{}_{\mu}
.

[
149
]

\mathcal{L}_{\textup{FP}}=\partial_{\mu}h_{\nu\rho}\partial^{\nu}h^{\mu\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\rho}h_{\mu\nu}\partial^{\rho}h^{\mu\nu}-\partial_{\mu}h^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}h+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}h\partial^{\mu}h-\frac{m^{2}}{2}\left(h^{\mu\nu}h_{\mu\nu}-h^{2}\right)\ .

(2.4.1)

Further ghost-like instabilities arise when interactions and non-linearities are added, especially in the presence of a nontrivial spacetime background. In massive gravity, this is known as the Boulware–Deser ghost
[
150
]
. In addition, aside from the ghost problem,
21
21

21

For more details on the problems of ghosts, we refer to
[
151
,
152
]
.
the couplings to gravity of the massive spin-2 field are inherently associated with low unitarity cutoffs. This has been proven in several settings of increasing generality, by studying the behavior of the elastic scattering amplitudes
\mathcal{A}(s)
for the massive spin-2 field in the limit of large center-of-mass energy
s

[
153
,
154
,
155
,
156
,
157
,
158
,
159
]
. Gravitational theories in which this amplitude has a power growth of the type
\mathcal{A}(s)\sim s^{\lambda}
will become strongly coupled at a scale

\Lambda_{\lambda}=\left(m^{\lambda-1}M_{\scalebox{0.6}{P}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\lambda}},

(2.4.2)

so that the higher the power
\lambda
, the narrower will be the window of validity of the effective field theory approximation. It has been shown that, while an arbitrary interacting theory will generically scale with
\Lambda_{5}
— this is for example the case of the Fierz–Pauli theory (
2.4.1
) coupled to gravity
[
153
]
— , specific choices of interactions, usually nonlinear and of the higher-derivative type, can raise the cutoff to higher energy scales. However, the cutoff of the theory is never greater than the scale
\Lambda_{3}
, which appears to be an insurmountable threshold for the couplings of a single massive spin-2 field. This has been shown to hold for nonlinear deformations of the Fierz–Pauli theory (
2.4.1
), as well as for interactions with gravity
[
157
]
and with fields of lower spins
[
158
,
159
]
. Theories that saturate the
\Lambda_{3}
threshold are therefore somewhat special. Indeed, as shown in
[
156
]
and
[
157
]
, this applies to the well-known ghost-free nonlinear theories of de Rham–Gabadadze–Tolley (dRGT) massive gravity
[
160
,
161
]
and Hassan–Rosen bigravity
[
162
]
.

We emphasize that these results for the scattering amplitudes and the corresponding strong coupling scales apply to theories with a single massive spin-2 field. By contrast, interactions involving additional massive spin-2 fields can raise the cutoff beyond the
\Lambda_{3}
scale
[
155
,
158
]
. For example, this is the case for compactified theories of gravity, in which the Kaluza–Klein interactions are tuned in such a way that the cutoff of the theory is, consistently, the higher-dimensional Planck scale. In order to reproduce this behavior, one must include the whole tower of Kaluza–Klein massive spin-2 modes
[
163
,
164
,
165
]
. It is therefore interesting to ask whether the only consistent effective field theories describing a single massive spin-2 field coupled to gravity are those that arise from higher-dimensional theories, or whether additional constructions are possible.

In
[
4
]
we investigate such a question in the case of supersymmetric theories. The supersymmetric
\mathcal{N}=1
multiplet of the massive spin-2 field includes, as superpartners, a massive spin-1 field and two massive Majorana spin-
\frac{3}{2}
fields,
22
22

22

This is a
\mathcal{N}=1
massive multiplets of the type discussed in Section
1.1.2
.
and the associated free theory was first constructed in
[
166
,
167
]
. Further studies on the self-interactions of this multiplet can be found in
[
168
,
169
,
170
]
, but less is known about explicit couplings to (super)gravity. A theory of this type that certainly exists is, as we emphasized, the one arising from a higher-dimensional theory through a proper Kaluza–Klein reduction. In this case, this theory is pure 5D supergravity
[
171
,
172
]
compactified on the orbifold
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, which yields, in four dimensions, pure supergravity coupled to a massless chiral multiplet and a tower of massive spin-2 multiplets. We explicitly perform this reduction in Appendix
C
. From the supersymmetric point of view, the question we address in
[
4
]
becomes whether the Kaluza–Klein coupling of a single massive spin-2 field to supergravity is universal or if more supersymmetric couplings are instead possible.

In fact, supersymmetry further restricts the spectrum of allowed couplings and, at the same time, provides a framework in which to identify them. This is given, as we pointed out at the beginning of the section, by the supercurrent superfield, which we construct explicitly for the massive spin-2 supermultiplet and use to probe its leading-order coupling to supergravity. The result is that supersymmetry and diffeomorphism invariance imply the existence of a
unique
consistent coupling of the massive spin-2 multiplet to new-minimal off-shell supergravity. The resulting theory includes non-minimal couplings to the gravitino and, in particular, to the metric field, both of which involve higher-derivative terms. Moreover, the interactions of the massive spin-2 field with gravity described by the theory are found to have an improved effective field theory cutoff, equal to
\Lambda_{4}
instead of
\Lambda_{5}
. Additional possible couplings can then be obtained by deforming this theory into one involving a coupling to non-minimal off-shell supergravity, as in eq. (
2.3.14
). We also argue that this class of couplings does not contain, by construction, the Kaluza–Klein theory. We now present this analysis and its results in full detail.

2.4.1
The massive spin-2 supermultiplet and its supercurrent superfield

We begin with the rigid
\mathcal{N}=1
supermultiplet of the massive spin-2 field, which we denote as

\left\{h_{\mu\nu},\lambda_{\mu},\chi_{\mu},A_{\mu}\right\}\ ,

(2.4.3)

where
h_{\mu\nu}
is the massive spin-2,
\lambda_{\mu}
and
\chi_{\mu}
are the Majorana spin-
\frac{3}{2}
, and
A_{\mu}
is the massive spin-1. At the linear level, they are related by supersymmetry as
23
23

23

The supersymmetry transformations (
2.4.4
) were first determined in
[
166
,
167
]
. Alternatively, one can extract them from the ones of pure 5D supergravity by compactification on the orbifold
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. We perform this computation explicitly in Appendix
C
.

\displaystyle\delta A_{\mu}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\lambda_{\mu}\ ,

(2.4.4)

\displaystyle\delta\lambda_{\mu}=-\frac{i}{4}\left(mh_{\mu\rho}+\tilde{F}_{\mu\rho}+2i\partial_{\rho}A_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\right)\gamma_{5}\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon\ ,

\displaystyle\delta h_{\mu\nu}=\frac{i}{m}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{5}\left(\partial_{(\mu}\lambda_{\nu)}-m\gamma_{(\mu}\chi_{\nu)}\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\delta\chi_{\mu}=\frac{i}{4}\partial_{\rho}h_{\mu\sigma}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\epsilon+\frac{1}{8m}\partial_{\mu}F_{\rho\sigma}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\epsilon+\frac{m}{4}A_{\rho}\left(2\delta^{\rho}_{\mu}+\gamma^{\rho}{}_{\mu}\right)\epsilon\ ,

where
m
is the mass of the multiplet. The theory associated with these transformations is the supersymmetric extension of the Fierz–Pauli lagrangian (
2.4.1
), given by
24
24

24

For convenience, we employ a non-canonical normalization for the bosonic fields. The normalization of the spin-1 field
A_{\mu}
differs from the canonical one by a factor
\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}
, whereas the one of the spin-2 field
h_{\mu\nu}
by a factor
\frac{1}{4}
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textup{SFP}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{8}F^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}-\frac{3}{4}m^{2}A^{\mu}A_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\lambda}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}\partial_{\nu}\lambda_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}\partial_{\nu}\chi_{\rho}-m\bar{\lambda}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu}\chi_{\nu}

(2.4.5)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\partial_{\mu}h_{\nu\rho}\partial^{\nu}h^{\mu\rho}-\frac{1}{8}\partial_{\rho}h_{\mu\nu}\partial^{\rho}h^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{\mu}h^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}h+\frac{1}{8}\partial_{\mu}h\partial^{\mu}h-\frac{m^{2}}{8}\left(h^{\mu\nu}h_{\mu\nu}-h^{2}\right)\ .

This lagrangian yields the equations of motion

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{\mu}F^{\mu\nu}-m^{2}A^{\nu}&=0,&\partial_{[\mu}F_{\nu\rho]}&=0,\\
\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}\partial_{\nu}\lambda_{\rho}+m\gamma^{\mu\nu}\chi_{\nu}&=0,&\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}\partial_{\nu}\chi_{\rho}+m\gamma^{\mu\nu}\lambda_{\nu}&=0,\end{aligned}

(2.4.6)

\displaystyle\Box h_{\mu\nu}-2\partial_{\rho}\partial_{(\mu}h^{\rho}{}_{\nu)}+\eta_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}h^{\rho\sigma}+\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}h-\eta_{\mu\nu}\Box h-m^{2}\left(h_{\mu\nu}-\eta_{\mu\nu}h\right)=0,

which can be expressed as the following set of dynamical equations and constraints:

\displaystyle\Box A_{\mu}=m^{2}A_{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\ ,

(2.4.7)

\displaystyle\Box h_{\mu\nu}=m^{2}h_{\mu\nu}\ ,

\displaystyle\partial^{\rho}h_{\rho\mu}

\displaystyle=0\ ,

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle=0\ ,

\displaystyle\cancel{\partial}\lambda_{\mu}

\displaystyle=m\chi_{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\Box\lambda_{\mu}=m^{2}\lambda_{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}\lambda_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\ ,

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mu}\lambda_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\ ,

\displaystyle\cancel{\partial}\chi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=m\lambda_{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\Box\chi_{\mu}=m^{2}\chi_{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}\chi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\ ,

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mu}\chi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\ .

Using these equations, one can check that the supersymmetry transformations (
2.4.4
) close, accordingly to the Super-Poincaré algebra (
1.1.7
)-(
1.2.6
), on

\left[\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\right]=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{2}\right)\partial_{\rho}\ .

(2.4.8)

We can now compute the supercurrent superfield
[
85
,
143
,
145
]
associated with this massive spin-2 field. For this purpose, we observe that the supersymmetry transformations (
2.4.4
) enjoy the following R-symmetry:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mu}\to e^{i\alpha\gamma_{5}}\lambda_{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\chi_{\mu}\to e^{-i\alpha\gamma_{5}}\chi_{\mu}\ ,

\displaystyle\epsilon\to e^{-i\alpha\gamma_{5}}\epsilon\ .

(2.4.9)

Thus, the massive spin-2 supermultiplet (
2.4.1
)-(
2.4.4
) is naturally associated with a supercurrent superfield of the R-multiplet type, given in eq. (
2.3.7
)-(
2.3.11
)
[
143
]
. As described in section
2.3
, this supercurrent superfield contains the R-symmetry conserved current as bottom component and closes on an antisymmetric two-form field. Therefore, we can explicitly construct this multiplet by starting with the Noether current of the R-symmetry (
2.4.9
) and applying successive supersymmetry transformations (
2.4.4
). These generate the remaining currents step by step, as dictated by the R-multiplet structure in (
2.3.11
). The R-symmetry current associated with eq. (
2.4.9
) is

J^{\mu}_{0}=\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\left(\bar{\lambda}_{\rho}\gamma_{\nu}\lambda_{\sigma}-\bar{\chi}_{\rho}\gamma_{\nu}\chi_{\sigma}\right)\ .

(2.4.10)

This R-symmetry current is indeed conserved on-shell and one can check that it satisfies the linear superfield condition (
2.3.1
). Following (
2.3.11
), its supersymmetry transformation (
2.4.4
) yields the current of supersymmetry
\Xi^{\mu}_{0}
, which results in

\displaystyle\Xi^{\mu}_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle\biggl\{\left(mh_{\rho\tau}+\tilde{F}_{\rho\tau}-2i\partial_{\tau}A_{\rho}\gamma_{5}\right)\gamma^{\tau}\gamma_{\nu}\lambda_{\sigma}

(2.4.11)

\displaystyle+\left[\partial_{\lambda}h_{\tau\rho}\gamma^{\lambda\tau}\gamma_{5}-\frac{i}{2m}\partial_{\rho}F_{\lambda\tau}\gamma^{\lambda\tau}+imA_{\tau}\left(2\delta^{\tau}_{\rho}+\gamma_{\rho}{}^{\tau}\right)\right]\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\chi_{\sigma}\biggr\}\ ,

which is also conserved on-shell. Then, a further supersymmetry transformations yields the antisymmetric field
X^{\mu\nu}_{0}=\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho}{}_{\sigma}\partial_{\rho}X_{0}^{\sigma}
, as in eq. (
2.3.10
), resulting in

X^{\mu}_{0}=\frac{1}{2}A_{\nu}\left(mh^{\nu\mu}+\tilde{F}^{\nu\mu}\right)+\frac{i}{4}\bar{\lambda}^{\nu}\gamma_{5}\gamma^{\mu}\lambda_{\nu}\ ,

(2.4.12)

as well the stress-energy tensor

T^{\mu\nu}_{0}=T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{K}}+\partial_{\rho}B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}+\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}\ .

(2.4.13)

Let us now focus on this energy-momentum tensor and describe the three terms into which it is organized. The term
T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{K}}
is the universal contribution from the kinetic terms of the various fields, associated the Proca, Rarita–Schwinger and Fierz–Pauli lagrangians:

T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{K}}=\frac{3}{2}T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{P}}+T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{RS}}+\frac{1}{4}T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{FP}}\ ,

(2.4.14)

with

\displaystyle T_{\textup{P}}^{\mu\nu}=F^{\mu\rho}F^{\nu}{}_{\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\eta^{\mu\nu}F^{2}+m^{2}A^{\mu}A^{\nu}-\frac{m^{2}}{2}\eta^{\mu\nu}A^{2}\ ,

(2.4.15)

\displaystyle T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{RS}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\lambda}^{\sigma}\gamma^{(\mu}\partial^{\nu)}\lambda_{\sigma}+\bar{\chi}^{\sigma}\gamma^{(\mu}\partial^{\nu)}\chi_{\sigma}\right)-\left(\bar{\lambda}^{\sigma}\gamma^{(\mu}\partial_{\sigma}\psi^{\nu)}+\bar{\chi}^{\sigma}\gamma^{(\mu}\partial_{\sigma}\chi^{\nu)}\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{FP}}=&\partial^{\mu}h^{\rho\sigma}\partial^{\nu}h_{\rho\sigma}+\partial^{\rho}h^{\mu\sigma}\partial_{\rho}h^{\nu}{}_{\sigma}-2\partial^{(\mu|}h^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}h^{|\nu)}{}_{\sigma}+m^{2}h^{\mu\rho}h^{\nu}{}_{\rho}\\
&+\frac{\eta^{\mu\nu}}{2}\left(-\partial^{\lambda}h^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\lambda}h_{\rho\sigma}+\partial^{\lambda}h^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}h_{\lambda\sigma}-m^{2}h^{\rho\sigma}h_{\rho\sigma}\right)\ .\end{aligned}

We remark that these tensors correspond to the Minkowski background limit of the standard, fully covariant tensors of the corresponding fields.
25
25

25

The complete stress-energy tensor of the covariantized Fierz–Pauli action can be found in
[
173
]
. The one for the Rarita–Schwinger field was instead reported here in eq. (
2.2.100
), referring to
[
2
]
.
Whereas for the Proca and Fierz–Pauli fields the Minkowski limit of the complete tensor coincide with the one obtained directly in flat space — namely, from the lagrangian (
2.4.5
) — via the standard Belinfante symmetrization procedure, for the Rarita–Schwinger one the two tensors differ by terms which come from the spin-connection of the original covariant derivatives. This can be checked explicitly working out the limit from the stress-energy tensor given in eq. (
2.2.100
). Despite being improvements from the flat space point of view
[
174
]
, these terms are crucial correctly interpreting the stress-energy tensor (
2.4.13
) and the associated coupling to gravity.

The terms
B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}
and
G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}
in eq. (
2.4.13
) are instead equal to

\displaystyle B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left(h^{(\nu}{}_{\sigma}\partial^{\mu)}h^{\rho\sigma}-\partial^{(\mu}h^{\nu)}{}_{\sigma}h^{\rho\sigma}\right)+\frac{5}{4}\left(A^{(\nu}\partial^{\mu)}A^{\rho}-A^{\rho}\partial^{(\mu}A^{\nu)}\right)

(2.4.16)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4m}\left(\partial^{(\mu}\tilde{F}^{\rho\sigma}h^{\nu)}{}_{\sigma}-\tilde{F}^{\rho\sigma}\partial^{(\mu}h^{\nu)}{}_{\sigma}+\tilde{F}^{(\nu}{}_{\sigma}\partial^{\mu)}h^{\rho\sigma}-\partial^{(\mu}\tilde{F}^{\nu)}{}_{\sigma}h^{\rho\sigma}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2m}\left(\partial^{(\mu}\bar{\chi}^{\nu)}\lambda^{\rho}-\bar{\chi}^{(\nu}\partial^{\mu)}\lambda^{\rho}+\partial^{(\mu}\bar{\lambda}^{\nu)}\chi^{\rho}-\bar{\lambda}^{(\nu}\partial^{\mu)}\chi^{\rho}\right)\;,

and
26
26

26

See Appendix
A
for the notation on the antisymmetrized indices used in eq. (
2.4.17
) and in the following.

\displaystyle G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\eta^{\nu][\sigma}F^{\mu]\lambda}F_{\lambda}{}^{[\rho}+\frac{1}{8m^{2}}\eta^{\nu[\sigma}\eta^{\mu]\rho}F^{2}+3\eta^{\nu][\sigma}A^{\mu]}A^{[\rho}+\frac{1}{4}\eta^{\nu[\mu}\eta^{\sigma]\rho}A^{2}

(2.4.17)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2m}\eta^{\rho][\mu}\left(\tilde{F}^{\sigma]}{}_{\lambda}h^{\lambda[\nu}-h^{\sigma]}{}_{\lambda}\tilde{F}^{\lambda[\nu}\right)-h^{\mu[\rho}h^{\nu]\sigma}+\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\rho][\mu}h^{\sigma]}{}_{\lambda}h^{\lambda[\nu}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m}\left(\bar{\lambda}^{[\sigma}\gamma^{\mu][\rho}\chi^{\nu]}+\bar{\lambda}^{[\rho}\gamma^{\nu][\sigma}\chi^{\mu]}\right)\;.

These two terms are improvements with respect to
T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{K}}
, namely deformations of the current that are trivially conserved and thus do not modify the conservation of the associated charges.
[
174
]
. They have in fact the following symmetry structures:
27
27

27

While the symmetry properties (
2.4.19
) are manifest for
\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}
in eq. (
2.4.17
), those of
\partial_{\rho}B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}
in eq. (
2.4.16
) require an on-shell manipulation to be manifest. Taking the Proca sector of eq. (
2.4.16
) as an example, one has

\displaystyle\frac{4}{5}\partial_{\rho}B_{A}^{\rho\mu\nu}=

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\left(A^{(\nu}\partial^{\mu)}A^{\rho}-A^{\rho}\partial^{(\mu}A^{\nu)}\right)=

(2.4.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\left(A^{\nu}\partial^{[\mu}A^{\rho]}+A^{[\mu}\partial^{\rho]}A^{\nu}+A^{[\mu|}\partial^{\nu}A^{|\rho]}\right)+\underbrace{\partial_{\rho}\left(A^{[\nu|}\partial^{\rho}A^{|\mu]}\right)}_{=0}=\frac{4}{5}\partial_{\rho}B_{A}^{[\rho\mu]\nu}\;.

Analogous manipulations yield also
\partial_{\rho}B^{\rho\mu\nu}=\partial_{\rho}B^{[\rho|\mu|\nu]}
.

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}=\partial_{\rho}B^{\rho(\mu\nu)}_{0}=\partial_{\rho}B^{[\rho\mu]\nu}_{0}=\partial_{\rho}B^{[\rho|\mu|\nu]}_{0}\ ,

(2.4.19)

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}=\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}G^{(\mu|\sigma|\nu)\rho}_{0}=\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}G^{[\mu\sigma]\nu\rho}_{0}=\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}G^{\mu\sigma[\nu\rho]}_{0}\ ,

The presence of improvements in the currents of the multiplet is rather standard
[
85
,
145
]
and they are perfectly consistent from the rigid theory point of view
[
174
]
. The peculiar property of the ones found in eq. (
2.4.16
)-(
2.4.17
) for the massive spin-2 supermultiplet is that they contain higher-derivative terms.

Therefore, we obtained this way the supercurrent superfield of the massive spin-2 supermultiplet (
2.4.4
), which is the fist result of
[
4
]
. This is given by

\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}=\left\{J^{\mu}_{0},\Xi^{\mu}_{0},T^{\mu\nu}_{0},X^{\mu\nu}_{0}\right\},

(2.4.20)

where
J^{\mu}_{0}
is the
R
-symmetry current (
2.4.10
) from which the multiplet was constructed, and the other currents defined respectively in (
2.4.11
), (
2.4.13
) and (
2.4.12
). The current multiplet
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
(
2.4.20
) is, by definition, a R-multiplet like eq. (
2.3.7
)-(
2.3.11
), and one can check that the supersymmetry transformations (
2.3.11
) of this multiplet close, on-shell, on (
2.4.8
).

2.4.2
The coupling to supergravity and its uniqueness

The motivation for constructing the supercurrent superfield
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
(
2.4.20
) was that it can be used to define the coupling to supergravity of the massive spin-2 multiplet (
2.4.3
)-(
2.4.4
), at leading order in the Planck mass. In particular, as we discussed in Section
2.3
, supercurrent superfields are dual to the off-shell formulations of supergravity. As an R-symmetry multiplet, the supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
(
2.4.20
) defines a coupling to the so-called new-minimal off-shell supergravity, introduced in eq. (
2.3.12
)
[
143
]
. The corresponding leading-order coupling lagrangian is given by

\mathcal{L}\sim-\frac{1}{2}(g_{\mu\nu}-\eta_{\mu\nu})T^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}\Xi^{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}V_{\mu}J^{\mu}+C_{\mu}X^{\mu}\;,

(2.4.21)

where
V_{\mu}
is an axial vector and
C_{\mu}
is the field strength of antisymmetric tensor field
C_{\mu}=\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial^{\nu}C^{\rho\sigma}
, as in eq. (
2.3.12
), while
g_{\mu\nu}
denotes here the fluctuation of the metric field around the Minkowski background.

When going from rigid to local supersymmetry, the currents of the supercurrent superfield are promoted to couplings to the gauge fields of supergravity. This is expressed by the leading-order coupling lagrangian (
2.4.21
). This means that also the improvements to such currents are not arbitrary anymore, but rather acquire physical significance as non-minimal couplings to the associated gauge fields
[
174
]
. Focusing on the stress-energy tensor
T^{\mu\nu}_{0}
of eq. (
2.4.13
), the improvement terms
B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}
of eq. (
2.4.16
) and
G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}
of eq. (
2.4.17
) should correspond to non-minimal couplings to the metric field of the new-minimal off-shell supergravity, as in eq. (
2.4.21
). Their specific form can be reconstructed from the symmetries (
2.4.19
) of the two improvement terms. Focusing on the linearised coupling of lagrangian (
2.4.21
), it follows that
G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}
defines a non-minimal coupling to the linearised Riemann tensor:

g_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}\approx\left(\partial_{\rho]}\partial_{[\sigma}g_{\mu][\nu}\right)G^{[\mu\sigma][\nu\rho]}_{0}\sim-\frac{1}{2}R_{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}_{0}\,,

(2.4.22)

according to

R_{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}\sim-2\partial_{\rho]}\partial_{[\sigma}g_{\mu][\nu}\ .

(2.4.23)

These non-minimal couplings are indeed peculiar, since the operator
G^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}
of eq. (
2.4.17
) contains higher-derivative terms, but they are perfectly consistent with the invariance under diffeomorphisms of the theory coupled to gravity. In contrast, the improvement
B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}
corresponds to a non-minimal coupling to the linearised Levi–Civita connection:

g_{\mu\nu}\partial_{\rho}B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}\approx\left(\partial_{[\rho}g_{\mu]\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\nu}g_{\mu\rho}\right)B^{[\rho\mu]\nu}_{0}\sim\Gamma_{\rho\mu\nu}B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}\ ,

(2.4.24)

as

\displaystyle g_{\rho\sigma}\Gamma^{\sigma}_{\mu\nu}\sim\partial_{[\mu}g_{\rho]\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\nu}g_{\mu\rho}\ .

(2.4.25)

Since these terms are couplings to the bare Levi–Civita connections — namely, not part of any covariant derivative/operator — they are in open contrast with diffeomorphism invariance. Therefore, because of these
B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}
-improvements in the stress-energy tensor
T^{\mu\nu}_{0}
(
2.4.13
), the supercurrent superfield
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
(
2.4.20
) does not define a consistent coupling to gravity of the massive spin-2 multiplet (
2.4.4
)
[
4
]
.

Although naturally associated with the massive spin-2 multiplet through the R-symmetry current
J^{\mu}_{0}
(
2.4.10
), it is necessary to deform the starting supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
(
2.4.20
) in order to look for consistent couplings to supergravity. Since the source of inconsistency are improvement terms in the stress-energy tensor
T^{\mu\nu}_{0}
, the strategy we employ in
[
4
]
is to study superfield improvements to the whole supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
, of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}\,\,\longrightarrow\,\,\mathcal{S}^{\mu}=\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}+\Delta\mathcal{S}^{\mu}\ ,

(2.4.26)

with

\displaystyle\Delta\mathcal{S}^{\mu}=\left\{\Delta J^{\mu},\Delta\Xi^{\mu},\Delta T^{\mu\nu},\Delta X^{\mu\nu}\right\}\ .

(2.4.27)

The idea is to find the most general supercurrent improvement
\Delta\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
such that the resulting stress-energy tensor
T^{\mu\nu}=T^{\mu\nu}_{0}+\Delta T^{\mu\nu}
does not contain any diffeomorphism breaking improvement terms of the
B^{\rho\mu\nu}
type (
2.4.16
). In superspace notation, this general improvement is written as

\Delta\mathcal{S}^{\mu}=\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{L}^{\mu\nu}+\sigma^{\mu\,\alpha\dot{\beta}}[D_{\alpha},\bar{D}_{\dot{\beta}}]\mathcal{U},

(2.4.28)

where
\mathcal{L}^{\mu\nu}=-\mathcal{L}^{\nu\mu}
is taken to be a linear superfield, namely satisfying the condition (
2.3.1
),

D^{2}\mathcal{L}^{\mu\nu}=0\ .

(2.4.29)

The superfield
\mathcal{U}
is instead real but unconstrained. Notice that the
\mathcal{L}^{\mu\nu}
-improvement still defines, by construction, a coupling to new-minimal off-shell supergravity (
2.3.12
). On the contrary, the
\mathcal{U}
-improvement breaks the conservation of the bottom component of
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}

[
145
]
. Such a supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
would be an example of the generalized supercurrent superfield introduced in eq. (
2.3.14
), which defines a coupling to a non-minimal formulation of off-shell supergravity
[
140
]
.

The unique coupling to new-minimal off-shell supergravity

We begin by analyzing the
\mathcal{U}=0
case,
i.e.
the improved couplings to new-minimal off-shell supergravity. As in the previous section constructing
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
, we begin with the bottom component of the linear improvement
\mathcal{L}^{\mu\nu}
, namely the axial improvement
\Delta J^{\mu}
of eq. (
2.4.26
). As for its superfield embedding, this improvement current has the form

\Delta J^{\mu}=\frac{1}{m}\partial_{\nu}L^{\mu\nu}\ ,

(2.4.30)

with
L^{\mu\nu}
being a real and antisymmetric composite operator. We restrict these operators to have a limited number of derivatives, in such a way as to produce contributions to the stress-energy tensor with at most four derivatives, as the ones characterizing
T^{\mu\nu}_{0}
in eq. (
2.4.13
), (
2.4.16
) and (
2.4.17
). With this working assumption, a general ansatz for
L^{\mu\nu}
can be written based on its consistency with the setup under consideration. First, the current
\Delta J^{\mu}
has to be an axial vector, and thus the operators entering
L^{\mu\nu}
have to be consistent with such a parity. This requirement is already quite stringent, and constraints
L^{\mu\nu}
to the following linear combination of independent operators:

\displaystyle L^{\mu\nu}=

\displaystyle c_{\textup{B1}}h^{[\mu}{}_{\lambda}\partial^{\lambda}A^{\nu]}+c_{\textup{B2}}h^{[\mu}{}_{\lambda}\partial^{\nu]}A^{\lambda}+\frac{c_{\textup{B3}}}{m}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\lambda}A_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}A^{\lambda}

(2.4.31)

\displaystyle+c_{\textup{F1}}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{\chi}_{\rho}\lambda_{\sigma}+ic_{\textup{F2}}\bar{\chi}^{[\mu}\gamma_{5}\lambda^{\nu]}.

Then, the second, essential requirement on
L^{\mu\nu}
is that it has to define, by construction, a linear multiplet, like the R-symmetry current of eq. (
2.4.10
). The linear multiplet constraint on the superfield
\mathcal{L}^{\mu\nu}
of eq. (
2.4.29
) translates on its bottom component to

\delta_{\textup{L}}^{2}L^{\mu\nu}=0\;,

(2.4.32)

where
\delta_{\textup{L}}
is the supersymmetry transformation with the left-handed spinor
\epsilon_{\textup{L}}
as parameter. This condition restricts further the linear combination of eq. (
2.4.31
). Using the Fierz identity

\epsilon_{\textup{L}}\bar{\epsilon}_{\textup{L}}=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{\textup{L}}\epsilon_{\textup{L}}\right)P_{\textup{L}},

(2.4.33)

the various operators of
L^{\mu\nu}
in eq. (
2.4.31
) transform as

\displaystyle\bullet

\displaystyle\delta_{\textup{L}}^{2}\left(h^{[\mu}{}_{\sigma}\partial^{\sigma}A^{\nu]}\right)=\frac{i}{4}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{\textup{L}}\epsilon_{\textup{L}}\right)\left[-\frac{1}{m}\partial_{\sigma}\bar{\lambda}^{[\mu}_{\textup{L}}\partial^{\nu]}\lambda^{\sigma}_{\textup{L}}+\bar{\chi}^{\sigma}_{\textup{R}}\gamma^{[\mu}\partial^{\nu]}\lambda_{\sigma\textup{L}}\right]\ ,

(2.4.34)

\displaystyle\bullet

\displaystyle\delta_{\textup{L}}^{2}\left(h^{[\mu}{}_{\sigma}\partial^{\nu]}A^{\sigma}\right)=\frac{i}{4}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{\textup{L}}\epsilon_{\textup{L}}\right)\left[\frac{1}{m}\partial_{\sigma}\bar{\lambda}^{[\mu}_{\textup{L}}\partial^{\nu]}\lambda^{\sigma}_{\textup{L}}+\bar{\chi}^{\sigma}_{\textup{R}}\gamma^{[\mu}\partial^{\nu]}\lambda_{\sigma\textup{L}}\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\bullet

\displaystyle\delta_{\textup{L}}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{m}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\lambda}A_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}A^{\lambda}\right)=\frac{i}{4}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{\textup{L}}\epsilon_{\textup{L}}\right)\left[\frac{2}{m}\partial_{\sigma}\bar{\lambda}^{[\mu}_{\textup{L}}\partial^{\nu]}\lambda^{\sigma}_{\textup{L}}-2\bar{\chi}^{\sigma}_{\textup{R}}\gamma^{[\mu}\partial^{\nu]}\lambda_{\sigma\textup{L}}\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\bullet

\displaystyle\delta_{\textup{L}}^{2}\left(\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{\chi}_{\rho}\lambda_{\sigma}\right)=0\ ,

\displaystyle\bullet

\displaystyle\delta_{\textup{L}}^{2}\left(\bar{\chi}^{[\mu}\gamma_{5}\lambda^{\nu]}\right)=0.

Thus, while the fermionic operators are consistent on their own with the linear multiplet structure, the bosonic ones are restricted to the combination given by

\displaystyle\quad c_{\textup{B2}}=0\ ,

\displaystyle c_{\textup{B1}}=2c_{\textup{B3}}\equiv 2c_{\textup{B}}.

(2.4.35)

Thus, the most general ansatz — at the four-derivative order in the associated stress-energy tensor — for the axial current (
2.4.30
) takes the compact form

L^{\mu\nu}=c_{\textup{B}}\left(2h^{[\mu|}{}_{\lambda}\partial^{\lambda}A^{|\nu]}+\frac{1}{m}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\lambda}A_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}A^{\lambda}\right)+c_{\textup{F1}}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{\chi}_{\rho}\lambda_{\sigma}+ic_{\textup{F2}}\bar{\chi}^{[\mu}\gamma_{5}\lambda^{\nu]}\ .

(2.4.36)

The linear superfield improvements
\mathcal{L}^{\mu\nu}
of eq. (
2.4.28
) are therefore spanned by the three real coefficients
\left\{c_{\textup{B}},c_{\textup{F1}},c_{\textup{F2}}\right\}
. The goal is to find the subset that corresponds to a diffeomorphism-invariant coupling of the stress-energy tensor
T^{\mu\nu}=T^{\mu\nu}_{0}+\Delta T^{\mu\nu}
. Applying, as before, the R-multiplet supersymmetry algebra (
2.3.11
), one finds that the three axial improvements in eq. (
2.4.36
) give the following
B^{\rho\mu\nu}
-like contributions to the associated energy-momentum tensors:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{\rho}\Delta B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{\textup{B}}=&\frac{1}{8}\partial_{\rho}\left(A^{(\mu}\partial^{\nu)}A^{\rho}-A^{\rho}\partial^{(\mu}A^{\nu)}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{8m^{2}}\partial_{\rho}\left(\partial^{\sigma}A^{(\mu}\partial_{\sigma}\partial^{\nu)}A^{\rho}-\partial_{\sigma}A^{\rho}\partial^{\sigma}\partial^{(\mu}A^{\nu)}\right),\end{aligned}

(2.4.37)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{\rho}\Delta B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{\textup{F1}}=&\frac{1}{2m}\partial_{\rho}\left(\partial^{(\mu}\bar{\chi}^{\nu)}\lambda^{\rho}-\bar{\chi}^{(\nu}\partial^{\mu)}\lambda^{\rho}+\partial^{(\mu}\bar{\lambda}^{\nu)}\chi^{\rho}-\bar{\lambda}^{(\nu}\partial^{\mu)}\chi^{\rho}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{2m}\partial_{\rho}\left(\partial^{(\mu|}\bar{\lambda}_{\sigma}\gamma^{\rho}\gamma^{|\nu)}\chi^{\sigma}-\partial^{\rho}\bar{\lambda}_{\sigma}\gamma^{(\mu}\gamma^{\nu)}\chi^{\sigma}\right),\end{aligned}

(2.4.38)

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\Delta B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{\textup{F2}}=-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{\rho}B^{\rho\mu\nu}_{0}.

(2.4.39)

From these equations, we see that the axial improvements dubbed with B and F1 in (
2.4.36
) lead to
new
non-diffeomorphism invariant improvements to the stress-energy tensor, and thus must be discarded. On the contrary, the improvement F2 is exactly proportional to the
B^{\rho\mu\nu}
-improvement (
2.4.16
) that affects the tensor
T^{\mu\nu}_{0}
of eq. (
2.4.13
). Hence, there is a solution for the coefficients in (
2.4.36
), given by

c_{\textup{B}}=c_{\textup{F1}}=0\;,\qquad c_{\textup{F2}}=4\;,

(2.4.40)

that satisfies the original requirement, namely that removes the non-diffeomorphism invariant terms from the R-symmetry current superfield
\mathcal{S}_{0}^{\mu}
(
2.4.20
) without introducing additional terms of the same type. This solution is
unique
, meaning that there exists only a single three-point coupling of the massive spin-2 supermultiplet (
2.4.4
) to new minimal off-shell supergravity (
2.3.12
) that is consistent with diffeomorphism invariance and supersymmetry at the same time. This result holds up to four derivatives operators, which are actually essential for realizing such a consistent coupling
[
4
]
.

The supercurrent supermultiplet that defines this unique coupling is

\mathcal{S}^{\mu}=\left\{J^{\mu},\Xi^{\mu},T^{\mu\nu},X^{\mu\nu}\right\},

(2.4.41)

with

\displaystyle J^{\mu}=\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\left(\bar{\lambda}_{\rho}\gamma_{\nu}\lambda_{\sigma}-\bar{\chi}_{\rho}\gamma_{\nu}\chi_{\sigma}\right)+\frac{4i}{m}\partial_{\nu}\left(\bar{\chi}^{[\mu}\gamma_{5}\lambda^{\nu]}\right)\;,

(2.4.42)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Xi^{\mu}=&\Xi^{\mu}_{0}-\frac{i}{m}\partial_{\nu}\biggl[\left(mh^{[\nu}{}_{\lambda}+\tilde{F}^{[\nu}{}_{\lambda}-2i\partial_{\lambda}A^{[\nu}\gamma_{5}\right)\gamma^{\lambda}\gamma_{5}\chi^{\mu]}\\
&+\left(\partial_{\rho}h^{[\mu}{}_{\sigma}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}-\frac{i}{2m}\partial^{[\mu}F_{\rho\sigma}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}+2imA^{[\mu}-imA_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu}\right)\lambda^{\nu]}\biggr]\;,\end{aligned}

(2.4.43)

\displaystyle T^{\mu\nu}=T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{K}}+\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}\mathcal{G}^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}\;,

(2.4.44)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} X^{\mu}=&\frac{i}{4}\bar{\lambda}^{\nu}\gamma_{5}\gamma^{\mu}\lambda_{\nu}+\frac{3m}{4}A_{\nu}h^{\nu\mu}+\frac{9}{8}A_{\nu}\tilde{F}^{\nu\mu}+\frac{1}{4m^{2}}\partial_{\rho}\partial_{\sigma}\left(\tilde{F}^{\mu\rho}A^{\sigma}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{8m}\partial_{\nu}\left(F^{\nu\rho}h^{\mu}{}_{\rho}-F^{\mu\rho}h^{\nu}{}_{\rho}\right)-\frac{3}{4m}\partial_{\nu}\left(\partial^{\mu}A^{\rho}h^{\nu}{}_{\rho}+\partial_{\rho}A^{\nu}h^{\rho\mu}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\nu}h^{\lambda}{}_{\rho}h_{\sigma\lambda}-\frac{1}{4m}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\left(\bar{\chi}_{\nu}\partial_{\rho}\lambda_{\sigma}+\bar{\lambda}_{\nu}\partial_{\rho}\chi_{\sigma}\right)\;,\end{aligned}

(2.4.45)

where
\Xi_{0}^{\mu}
and
T^{\mu\nu}_{\textup{K}}
are, respectively, the current of supersymmetry of the minimal R-multiplet (
2.4.11
)-(
2.4.20
) and the Hilbert stress-energy tensor (
2.4.15
). The main feature of the resulting coupling to gravity — namely, the stress-energy tensor in eq. (
2.4.44
) — is the following non-minimal coupling to the Riemann tensor:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}h^{\rho[\mu}h^{\sigma]\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\eta^{\rho][\mu}h^{\sigma]}{}_{\lambda}h^{\lambda[\nu}+2\eta^{\rho][\mu}A^{\sigma]}A^{[\nu}-\frac{3}{4m^{2}}F^{\mu\sigma}F^{\nu\rho}+\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\eta^{\rho][\mu}F^{\sigma]}{}_{\lambda}F^{\lambda[\nu}

(2.4.46)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left(\eta^{\sigma][\nu}F^{\rho]}{}_{\lambda}\partial^{\lambda}A^{[\mu}+\eta^{\rho][\mu}F^{\sigma]}{}_{\lambda}\partial^{\lambda}A^{[\nu}\right)+\frac{1}{2m}\eta^{\rho][\mu}\left(\tilde{F}^{\sigma]}{}_{\lambda}h^{\lambda[\nu}-h^{\sigma]}{}_{\lambda}\tilde{F}^{\lambda[\nu}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2m}\left(\varepsilon^{\mu\sigma\lambda\tau}A_{\lambda}\partial^{[\nu}h^{\rho]}{}_{\tau}+\varepsilon^{\nu\rho\lambda\tau}A_{\lambda}\partial^{[\mu}h^{\sigma]}{}_{\tau}\right)+\frac{1}{m}\left(\bar{\lambda}^{[\sigma}\gamma^{\mu][\rho}\chi^{\nu]}+\bar{\lambda}^{[\rho}\gamma^{\nu][\sigma}\chi^{\mu]}\right)\;.

As stressed, this coupling contains higher-derivative terms, which are required by the consistency of the theory with supersymmetry — as operators of this type occurred already in the minimal setup (
2.4.13
)-(
2.4.17
) — as well as with diffeomorphism invariance
[
4
]
.

Starting from the supercurrent (
2.4.41
) and the linear order coupling to supergravity (
2.4.21
), we can extrapolate a nonlinear, covariant lagrangian that reduces to the described couplings in the proper limit. This is given by the terms

\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{\textup{SUGRA}}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{SFP}}+\mathcal{L}_{\psi}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{non-min}}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{quartic}}+\dots\;,

(2.4.47)

which we now proceed to describe. The term
\mathcal{L}_{\textup{SUGRA}}
is the standard pure supergravity lagrangian of eq. (
2.1.14
). The term
\mathcal{L}_{\text{SFP}}
is the minimal covariantization of the supersymmetric Fierz–Pauli lagrangian of eq. (
2.4.5
), equal to

\displaystyle e^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\textup{SFP}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{8}F^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}-\frac{3}{4}m^{2}A^{\mu}A_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\lambda}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}D_{\nu}\lambda_{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}D_{\nu}\chi_{\rho}-m\bar{\lambda}_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu\nu}\chi_{\nu}

(2.4.48)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\nabla_{\mu}h_{\nu\rho}\nabla^{\nu}h^{\mu\rho}-\frac{1}{8}\nabla_{\rho}h_{\mu\nu}\nabla^{\rho}h^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}\nabla_{\mu}h^{\mu\nu}\nabla_{\nu}h+\frac{1}{8}\nabla_{\mu}h\nabla^{\mu}h-\frac{m^{2}}{8}\left(h^{\mu\nu}h_{\mu\nu}-h^{2}\right)\;.

Next, the term
\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
encodes all the couplings to the gravitino, namely both the minimal coupling to the supersymmetry current
\Xi^{\mu}
of eq. (
2.4.11
) and the non-minimal one defined by the improvement term in eq. (
2.4.43
):

\displaystyle e^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\psi}=-\frac{e^{-1}}{4}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\bar{\psi}_{\mu}

\displaystyle\biggl[\left(mh_{\rho\tau}+\tilde{F}_{\rho\tau}-2i\nabla_{\tau}A_{\rho}\gamma_{5}\right)\gamma^{\tau}\gamma_{\nu}\lambda_{\sigma}

(2.4.49)

\displaystyle+\left(\nabla_{\lambda}h_{\tau\rho}\gamma^{\lambda\tau}\gamma_{5}-\frac{i}{2m}\nabla_{\rho}F_{\lambda\tau}\gamma^{\lambda\tau}+imA_{\tau}\left(2\delta^{\tau}_{\rho}+\gamma_{\rho}{}^{\tau}\right)\right)\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}\chi_{\sigma}\biggr]

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2m}\bar{\rho}_{\mu\nu}\biggl[

\displaystyle\left(mh^{\nu}{}_{\lambda}+\tilde{F}^{\nu}{}_{\lambda}-2i\nabla_{\lambda}A^{\nu}\gamma_{5}\right)\gamma^{\lambda}\gamma_{5}\chi^{\mu}

\displaystyle+\left(\nabla_{\rho}h^{\mu}{}_{\sigma}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}-\frac{i}{2m}\nabla^{\mu}F_{\rho\sigma}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}+2imA^{\mu}-imA_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\mu}\right)\lambda^{\nu}\biggr]\;,

where we introduced the gravitino field-strength
\rho_{\mu\nu}=D_{\mu}\psi_{\nu}-D_{\nu}\psi_{\mu}
. Instead, the non-minimal couplings to the metric field are all cast into the term
\mathcal{L}_{\text{non-min}}
, equal to

\displaystyle e^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\text{non-min}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}R_{\mu\sigma\nu\rho}\left[-\frac{1}{2}h^{\rho\mu}h^{\sigma\nu}+\frac{1}{2}g^{\rho\mu}h^{\sigma}{}_{\lambda}h^{\lambda\nu}+2g^{\rho\mu}A^{\sigma}A^{\nu}-\frac{3}{4m^{2}}F^{\mu\sigma}F^{\nu\rho}+\frac{1}{2m^{2}}g^{\rho\mu}F^{\sigma}{}_{\lambda}F^{\lambda\nu}\right.

(2.4.50)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2}{m^{2}}g^{\sigma\nu}F^{\rho}{}_{\lambda}\nabla^{\lambda}A^{\mu}+\frac{e^{-1}}{m}\varepsilon^{\mu\sigma\lambda\tau}A_{\lambda}\nabla^{\nu}h^{\rho}{}_{\tau}+\frac{1}{m}g^{\rho\mu}\tilde{F}^{\sigma}{}_{\lambda}h^{\lambda\nu}+\frac{2}{m}\bar{\lambda}^{\sigma}\gamma^{\mu\rho}\chi^{\nu}\right]\;.

Finally, the term
\mathcal{L}_{\textup{quartic}}
contains terms quartic in the fields, arising from integrating out the auxiliary fields
V_{\mu}
and
C_{\mu\nu}
in the minimal coupling lagrangian (
2.4.21
), which yields a leftover term equal to
-\frac{1}{4}X_{\mu}J^{\mu}
. The associated eq. (
2.4.45
) and (
2.4.42
) give

\displaystyle e^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\text{quartic}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\bigg(e^{-1}\varepsilon_{\mu}{}^{\nu\rho\sigma}\left(\bar{\lambda}_{\rho}\gamma_{\nu}\lambda_{\sigma}-\bar{\chi}_{\rho}\gamma_{\nu}\chi_{\sigma}\right)+\frac{4i}{m}\nabla^{\nu}\left(\bar{\chi}_{[\mu}\gamma_{5}\lambda_{\nu]}\right)\bigg)\bigg[\frac{i}{4}\bar{\lambda}^{\tau}\gamma_{5}\gamma^{\mu}\lambda_{\tau}+\frac{3m}{4}A_{\tau}h^{\tau\mu}

(2.4.51)

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{8}A_{\tau}\tilde{F}^{\tau\mu}+\frac{1}{8m}\nabla_{\tau}\left(F^{\tau\lambda}h^{\mu}{}_{\lambda}-F^{\mu\lambda}h^{\tau}{}_{\lambda}\right)-\frac{3}{4m}\nabla_{\tau}\left(\nabla^{\mu}A^{\lambda}h^{\tau}{}_{\lambda}+\nabla_{\lambda}A^{\tau}h^{\lambda\mu}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4m^{2}}\nabla_{\lambda}\nabla_{\pi}\left(\tilde{F}^{\mu\lambda}A^{\pi}\right)+\frac{e^{-1}}{4}\varepsilon^{\mu\tau\lambda\pi}\nabla_{\tau}h^{\alpha}{}_{\lambda}h_{\pi\alpha}-\frac{e^{-1}}{4m}\varepsilon^{\mu\tau\lambda\pi}\left(\bar{\chi}_{\tau}D_{\lambda}\lambda_{\pi}+\bar{\lambda}_{\tau}D_{\lambda}\chi_{\pi}\right)\bigg]\;.

The dots in the total lagrangian of eq. (
2.4.47
) denote further terms of higher-order in the number of fields which the analysis we carried out does not capture. Indeed, the supercurrent superfield
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
of eq. (
2.4.41
) define a leading-order coupling, in which the theory is exact, but is insensitive to higher-order ones. The only couplings of this type that we can determine are those collected in the
\mathcal{L}_{\text{quartic}}
term, which still arise from the quadratic off-shell lagrangian.

The non-minimal deformations

The analysis of the previous section showed that there exists a unique coupling of the massive spin-2 supermultiplet (
2.4.4
) to new-minimal off-shell supergravity (
2.3.12
). This is given by the supercurrent superfield
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
of eq. (
2.4.41
), which was obtained by properly improving the minimal supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
(
2.4.20
) via the linear improvement superfield
\mathcal{L}^{\mu\nu}
, given by eq. (
2.4.28
)-(
2.4.30
) and (
2.4.36
)-(
2.4.40
). As we stressed, this is a coupling to new-minimal off-shell supergravity, since the supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
is, by construction, an R-multiplet (
2.3.7
)-(
2.3.11
), and it is unique at the four-derivative order.

Possible extensions of such a coupling can be found by deforming the new-minimal setup through the
\mathcal{U}
-term in the general improvement superfield of eq. (
2.4.28
). The bottom component of this
\mathcal{U}
-improvement term yields a contribution to the axial current that is not conserved
[
145
]
, so that the coupling to supergravity defined by the resulting supercurrent multiplet is of the non-minimal type
[
140
]
.

This non-minimal improvement yields the following contributions to the stress energy tensor and supersymmetry current:

T^{\mu\nu}\rightarrow T^{\mu\nu}+(\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}-\eta^{\mu\nu}\Box)U\;,\quad\Xi^{\mu}\rightarrow\Xi^{\mu}-2\gamma^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\nu}\Upsilon\;

(2.4.52)

where

\displaystyle U=\mathcal{U}|_{\vartheta=0}\ ,

\displaystyle\delta U=\bar{\epsilon}\Upsilon\;.

(2.4.53)

Hence, the
\mathcal{U}
-improvement contributes to the lagrangian with non-minimal couplings of the type
[
145
]

e^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{U}}=-\frac{1}{2}R\,U+\bar{\rho}_{\mu\nu}\gamma^{\mu\nu}\Upsilon\;,

(2.4.54)

Similarly to the new-minimal case, the operator
U
is a polynomial of composite operators quadratic in the number of fields and containing at most two derivatives. However, it cannot be used to remove the non-diffeomorphism invariant operator
B^{\rho\mu\nu}
of eq. (
2.4.16
) that affects the minimal supercurrent superfield
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}_{0}
(
2.4.20
).

We therefore conclude that the unique new-minimal coupling defined by the supercurrent superfield
S^{\mu}
found in eq. (
2.4.41
) is the core, characteristic element of a class of couplings of the massive spin-2 field (
2.4.4
) to supergravity. The various elements of this set are obtained by non-minimal improvements to the new-minimal supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
, and the corresponding lagrangians differ only in the couplings to the Ricci scalar and gravitino field strength as in eq. (
2.4.54
)
[
4
]
.

2.4.3
The Kaluza–Klein supergravity and the Stückelberg symmetry

The outcome of the above analysis — which is the main result of
[
4
]
— is that the coupling to supergravity of the massive spin-2 supermultiplet (
2.4.4
) is unique (up to non-minimal improvement terms (
2.4.54
)) and contains 4-derivative operators. On the other hand, as mentioned at the beginning of this chapter, one such coupling that certainly exists is the one originating from pure supergravity in five dimensions, compactified on the orbifold
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. From the point of view of the off-shell formulations of supergravity, the action of the orbifold projection on the auxiliary fields of the minimal off-shell supergravity in five dimensions
[
175
]
predicts that the four-dimensional compactified theory should be realized as a non-minimal off-shell supergravity
[
140
]
. However, this Kaluza–Klein theory is clearly different from the one that could be obtained from the non-minimal
\mathcal{U}
-type deformations (
2.4.30
)-(
2.4.54
) of the supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
(
2.4.41
), since the latter necessarily contains higher-derivative operators, whereas the former does not. The source of this apparent contradiction lies in the underlying assumptions of the analysis described so far, which we now discuss.

The unique coupling of the massive spin-2 supermultiplet to new-minimal off-shell supergravity defined by the supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
of eq. (
2.4.41
) — and similarly for its non-minimal deformations — was constructed step by step, following the transformation rules of the R-symmetry supercurrent superfield (
2.3.11
). Performing such a computation implicitly assumes that the supersymmetry transformations of the massless supergravity multiplet, dual to the supermultiplet of currents, are the ones of the new-minimal off-shell formulations (
2.3.12
) and do not get modified. However, this assumption is nontrivial, since it is not guaranteed a priori that the coupling of a massive multiplet to supergravity does not require that the supersymmetry transformations of the massless fields be modified, even at leading order.

This is precisely what happens to the coupling of the massive spin-2 multiplet of eq. (
2.4.3
) to gravity in the context of the Kaluza–Klein theory. The reason is that in the compactified theories, the degrees of freedom making up the massive gauge fields of the spin-2 supermultiplet of eq. (
2.4.3
) naturally appear in Stückelberg form,
28
28

28

For a review on the Stückelberg formulation of massive spin-2 fields, see for example
[
152
]
. For the case of massive spin-
\frac{3}{2}
, see instead
[
176
,
167
]
.
since the Stückelberg symmetry relating the massive gauge fields’ degrees of freedom arises as the massive Kaluza–Klein modes of the higher-dimensional gauge symmetries. The formulation in which the fields organize as in eq. (
2.4.3
) is the unitary gauge of the Stückelberg symmetry, in which the pure gauge degrees of freedom are effectively set to zero. However, in order for the supersymmetry transformations of the system to preserve this unitary gauge, the transformations of the massless Kaluza–Klein fields must be modified at the linearised level.

Let us show this explicitly for the pure 5D supergravity algebra compactified on the orbifold
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. This compactification is performed in Appendix
C
, to which we refer for all details. The pure 5D supergravity multiplet contains the fünfbein
E_{M}{}^{A}
, a vector field
\mathcal{A}_{M}
and a symplectic Majorana gravitino
\Psi_{M}
. To study the dimensional reduction on
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, we start from the compactification ansatz, in the language of differential forms,

\displaystyle E^{a}

\displaystyle=\phi^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{a}\;,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle=a\left(dy+B\right)+A\;,

(2.4.55)

\displaystyle E^{4}

\displaystyle=\phi\left(dy+B\right)\;,

\displaystyle\Psi

\displaystyle=\phi^{\frac{5}{4}}\,\zeta\left(dy+B\right)+\phi^{-\frac{1}{4}}\left(\psi-\frac{1}{2}e^{a}\gamma_{a}\gamma_{5}\zeta\right)\;.

Under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
parity on the
S^{1}
coordinate
y
, the bosonic fields
e_{\mu}{}^{a}
,
\phi
and
a
are even and
A_{\mu}
and
B_{\mu}
are odd, while the fermions are such that

\psi{}_{\mu}(-y)=\sigma_{3}\gamma_{5}\psi{}_{\mu}(y)\;,\qquad\zeta(-y)=-\sigma_{3}\gamma_{5}\zeta(y)\;,

(2.4.56)

where the action of the Pauli matrix
\sigma_{3}
is related to the 5D R-symmetry. The massive Kaluza–Klein modes of the fields
B_{\mu}
,
\phi
,
a
and
\zeta
are the pure Stückelberg degrees of freedom. The unitary gauge is effectively imposed by setting these fields to zero, namely

\displaystyle B_{\mu}(x,y)=0\;,

\displaystyle\phi(x,y)=\phi(x)\;,

\displaystyle a(x,y)=a(x)\;,

\displaystyle\zeta(x,y)=\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(1-\sigma_{3}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\zeta(x)\;.

(2.4.57)

On the other hand, these fields transform nontrivially under supersymmetry. Focusing on the fields
B_{\mu}
and
\phi
— related to the massive spin-2 — and including a 5D diffeomorphism of parameter
\xi^{M}(x,y)
, these are given by

\displaystyle\delta B_{\mu}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(x)^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{2}\bar{\epsilon}(x)\gamma_{5}\psi_{\mu}(x,y)+\phi(x)^{-3}g_{\mu\nu}(x,y)\partial_{y}\xi^{\nu}(x,y)+\partial_{\mu}\xi^{5}(x,y)\ ,

(2.4.58)

\displaystyle\delta\phi(x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi(x)}{2}\bar{\epsilon}(x)\gamma_{5}\zeta(x)+\xi^{\mu}(x,y)\partial_{\mu}\phi(x)+\partial_{y}\xi^{5}(x,y)\phi(x)\;.

Thus, in order for the unitary gauge (
2.4.57
) and these supersymmetry transformations to be consistent with each other, one must set the diffeomorphism parameters to be

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\xi^{5}(x,y)

\displaystyle=-\xi^{\mu}(x,y)\partial_{\mu}\log\phi(x)\;,

(2.4.59)

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\xi^{\mu}(x,y)+\phi(x)^{3}g^{\mu\nu}(x,y)\partial_{\nu}\xi^{5}(x,y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\phi(x)^{\frac{3}{2}}}{2}g^{\mu\nu}(x,y)\bar{\epsilon}(x)\gamma_{5}\psi_{\nu}(x,y)\ .

In turn, the supersymmetry variation of the vierbein
e_{\mu}{}^{a}
becomes

\delta e_{\mu}{}^{a}(x,y)=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}(x)\gamma^{a}\psi_{\mu}(x,y)+D_{\mu}\bigl(\xi^{\nu}(x,y)e_{\nu}{}^{a}(x,y)\bigr)+\xi^{5}(x,y)\partial_{y}e_{\mu}{}^{a}(x,y)\;,

(2.4.60)

from which one sees that the massless vierbein transformation is modified at leading order, with terms quadratic in the massive fields, involving additional derivatives. This deformation cannot be reabsorbed by a local redefinition in the unitary gauge, since also the closure of the massless supersymmetry algebra gets modified, with respect to eq. (
2.4.8
), with terms quartic in the massive modes:

\xi^{\mu}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\sim-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\gamma^{\mu}\epsilon_{2}+\frac{1}{4m^{2}}\bar{\epsilon}_{1}h^{\nu\rho}\psi_{\rho}\partial_{\nu}\bigl(\bar{\epsilon}_{2}h^{\mu\sigma}\psi_{\sigma}\bigr)-\frac{1}{4m^{2}}\bar{\epsilon}_{2}h^{\nu\rho}\psi_{\rho}\partial_{\nu}\bigl(\bar{\epsilon}_{1}h^{\mu\sigma}\psi_{\sigma}\bigr)\;.

(2.4.61)

Note that repeating a similar analysis for the spin-
\frac{3}{2}
field and the massive modes of the supersymmetry parameter, the massless transformations of the 4D gravitino would also be modified, but this time by terms that can be reabsorbed by field redefinitions in the unitary gauge. The nontrivial deformation of the massless supersymmetry transformation is a feature and a consequence of the spin-2 field only
[
4
]
.

Therefore, the Kaluza–Klein theory cannot be retrieved assuming both the Stückelberg unitary gauge and the undeformed supersymmetry transformations of the massless off-shell supergravity multiplet. One can either work outside the unitary gauge, keeping the Stückelberg degrees of freedom as additional fields, and the undeformed massless supersymmetry algebra, or impose the unitary gauge and work in a setup in which the whole supersymmetry transformations are deformed. The analysis performed in the previous sections is clearly based on the use of the unitary gauge for the massive spin-2 multiplet gauge fields, as in eq. (
2.4.3
)-(
2.4.4
) and the undeformed supersymmetry transformations of the massless off-shell supergravity. This implies, on one hand, that the Kaluza–Klein theory is not captured by such a setup. On the other hand, it also means that the unique coupling to new-minimal supergravity described by the supercurrent superfield
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
of eq. (
2.4.41
) is a genuinely additional coupling of the massive spin-2 multiplet to supergravity
[
4
]
.

2.4.4
The
{2\to 2}
massive spin-2 scattering amplitude

We now analyze in more detail the unique class of couplings of the massive spin-2 supermultiplet to off-shell supergravity discussed in the previous sections. From the leading-order coupling described by the supercurrent superfield of eq. (
2.4.26
)–(
2.4.28
), we can compute the
2\to 2
elastic scattering amplitude of the massive spin-2 field
h_{\mu\nu}
. In the theory under consideration, this amplitude receives contributions only from the stress-energy tensor. In general, this is given by the new-minimal coupling tensor
T^{\mu\nu}
of eq. (
2.4.44
), plus the possible non-minimal extensions like eq. (
2.4.52
)–(
2.4.54
). We emphasize that this amplitude is calculated at the three-point level,
i.e.
at leading order in the Planck mass
M_{\scalebox{0.6}{P}}
, where the defining supercurrent is exact. The amplitude is generally sensitive to corrections from higher-order interactions, such as four-point couplings of the massive spin-2. These couplings are, however, not captured by the current setup.

We initially focus, as before, on the unique coupling to new-minimal supergravity, given by the supercurrent
\mathcal{S}^{\mu}
of eq. (
2.4.41
). From the associated stress-energy tensor
T^{\mu\nu}
of eq. (
2.4.44
), one reads the following three-point function:

\displaystyle\includegraphics[scale]{Images/3pf.png}

(2.4.62)

This three-point function defines the tree-level amplitude

i(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{\scalebox{0.6}{(4)}}(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{1}^{\prime}-p_{2}^{\prime})\mathcal{A}=-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\langle{\rm out}|\int d^{4}xd^{4}y:T^{\mu\nu}_{h}(x)D_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(x-y)T^{\rho\sigma}_{h}(y):|{\rm in}\rangle\;,

(2.4.63)

where we have reinstated the Planck mass factor as
\kappa^{2}=8\pi M_{\scalebox{0.6}{P}}^{-2}
and
D_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
is the graviton propagator, which we define in momentum space as

D_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(p)=-\frac{i}{2p^{2}}\left(\eta_{\mu\rho}\eta_{\nu\sigma}+\eta_{\mu\sigma}\eta_{\nu\rho}-\eta_{\mu\nu}\eta_{\rho\sigma}\right)\ .

(2.4.64)

The general amplitude (
2.4.63
) results in the standard sum of the
s
,
t
and
u
-channel

\begin{aligned} \includegraphics[scale]{Images/stu_channels.png}\end{aligned}.

(2.4.65)

We now compute this amplitude explicitly. We work in momentum space, where the momentum
p^{\mu}=\left(E_{\bm{p}},\bm{p}\right)
is parameterized as

\displaystyle E_{\bm{p}}=\sqrt{\bm{p}^{2}+m^{2}},

\displaystyle\bm{p}=\lvert\bm{p}\rvert\left(\sin\theta\cos\phi,\sin\theta\sin\phi,\cos\theta\right)\equiv\lvert\bm{p}\rvert\hat{\bm{p}}\ ,

(2.4.66)

with
\left(\theta,\phi\right)
being the angles with respect to the
z
-axis. The expansion of the massive spin-2 field in momentum space is
29
29

29

The factor of
2
in the expansion of eq. (
2.4.67
) reinstates the canonical normalization of the spin-2 field
h_{\mu\nu}
with respect to the non-canonical one chosen in eq. (
2.4.5
)-(
2.4.48
).

h_{\mu\nu}(x)=2\int\frac{d^{3}p}{\left(2\pi\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{2E_{{\bm{p}}}}}\sum_{{\sigma}}\left(\epsilon_{\mu\nu}\left({\sigma},\bm{p}\right)e^{ip\cdot x}a_{{\sigma},\bm{p}}+\bar{\epsilon}_{\mu\nu}\left({\sigma},\bm{p}\right)e^{-ip\cdot x}a_{{\sigma},\bm{p}}^{\dagger}\right)\ ,

(2.4.67)

where
\{a_{{\sigma},\bm{p}},a_{{\sigma},\bm{p}}^{\dagger}\}
are the ladder operators and
\epsilon_{\mu\nu}({\sigma},\bm{p})
are the polarization functions. They depend on the space momentum
\bm{p}
and on the five polarization states
{\sigma}=\left\{\pm 2,\pm 1,0\right\}
of the massive spin-2 field. The polarization functions satisfy the following orthogonality and completeness relations:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu}({\sigma},\bm{p})\bar{\epsilon}_{\mu\nu}(\sigma^{\prime},\bm{p})=\delta_{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}},

(2.4.68)

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma}\epsilon^{\mu\nu}({\sigma},\bm{p})\bar{\epsilon}^{\rho\sigma}({\sigma},\bm{p})=\frac{1}{2}\left(P^{\mu\rho}P^{\nu\sigma}+P^{\nu\rho}P^{\nu\sigma}\right)-\frac{1}{3}P^{\mu\nu}P^{\rho\sigma}\ ,

where
\textstyle{P^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}+\frac{p^{\mu}p^{\nu}}{m^{2}}}
. Moreover, the equations of motion (
2.4.6
)-(
2.4.7
) impose

\displaystyle p^{\mu}\epsilon_{\mu\nu}({\sigma},\bm{p})=0,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu}{}_{\mu}({\sigma},\bm{p})=0\ .

(2.4.69)

According to these general properties, we choose the polarization functions to be
[
163
,
164
]

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu}\left({\pm 2},\bm{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{\mu}_{\pm 1}(\bm{p})\epsilon^{\nu}_{\pm 1}(\bm{p}),

(2.4.70)

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu}\left(\pm 1,\bm{p}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\epsilon^{\mu}_{\pm 1}(\bm{p})\epsilon^{\nu}_{0}(\bm{p})+\epsilon^{\nu}_{\pm 1}(\bm{p})\epsilon^{\mu}_{0}(\bm{p})\right),

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu}(0,\bm{p})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\left(\epsilon^{\mu}_{1}(\bm{p})\epsilon^{\nu}_{-1}(\bm{p})+\epsilon^{\nu}_{1}(\bm{p})\epsilon^{\mu}_{-1}(\bm{p})+2\epsilon^{\mu}_{0}(\bm{p})\epsilon^{\nu}_{0}(\bm{p})\right),

with

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\pm 1}^{\mu}(\bm{p})=

\displaystyle\pm\frac{e^{\pm i\phi}}{\sqrt{2}}\left(0,-\cos\theta\cos\phi\pm i\sin\phi,-\cos\theta\sin\phi\mp i\cos\phi,\sin\theta\right),

(2.4.71)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}^{\mu}(\bm{p})=

\displaystyle\frac{E_{\bm{p}}}{m}\left(\sqrt{1-\frac{m^{2}}{E_{\bm{p}}^{2}}},\hat{\bm{p}}\right).

These are all the tools needed to compute the scattering amplitude (
2.4.63
)-(
2.4.65
). This involves in general four arbitrary helicity states for the external massive spin-2 fields, of the form
\mathcal{A}\left(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\right)
. In the absence of the improvement term produced by supersymmetry, the most divergent of the combinations is the elastic helicity-0 scattering
[
163
,
164
]
. In the present case, this scattering amplitude is found to be
[
4
]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(0,0,0,0)

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa^{2}}{16m^{4}s(s-4m^{2})}\left[s^{5}\sin^{2}\theta\left(3+\cos^{2}\theta\right)-\frac{4m^{2}s^{4}}{3}(21-29\cos^{2}\theta)\right.

(2.4.72)

\displaystyle+2m^{4}s^{3}\left(\frac{32}{\sin^{2}\theta}+\frac{37-231\cos^{2}\theta+18\cos^{4}\theta}{3}\right)

\displaystyle-16m^{6}s^{2}\left(\frac{16}{\sin^{2}\theta}-7+2\cos^{2}\theta-\cos^{4}\theta\right)

\displaystyle\left.+32m^{8}s\,\left(\frac{4}{\sin^{2}\theta}+\frac{11+26\cos^{2}\theta}{3}\right)+128m^{10}\cos(2\theta)\right]

In the Regge limit of large
s
at fixed
t
, this amplitude grows as
\mathcal{A}\left(0,0,0,0\right)\sim s^{2}
, while in the high-energy limit of large
s
and fixed angle
\theta
it scales as
\mathcal{A}\left(0,0,0,0\right)\sim s^{3}
. Thus, this amplitude saturates the
\Lambda_{3}
threshold discussed in
[

,

,

,

,

,

,
