\displaystyle=-\chi_{\alpha}\psi^{\alpha}=\chi\psi\ ,

(A.1.8)

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\theta}^{\dot{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\theta}^{2}\epsilon^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}\bar{\chi}=\bar{\psi}_{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\chi}^{\dot{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=-\bar{\chi}^{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\psi}_{\dot{\alpha}}=\bar{\chi}\bar{\psi}\ ,

\displaystyle\theta_{\alpha}\theta_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\theta^{2}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\theta\sigma^{m}\bar{\psi}\right)\left(\theta\sigma^{n}\bar{\chi}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\theta^{2}\left(\bar{\psi}\bar{\sigma}^{m}\sigma^{n}\bar{\chi}\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\beta}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\theta}^{2}\epsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\psi\sigma^{m}\bar{\theta}\right)\left(\chi\sigma^{n}\bar{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\theta}^{2}\left(\psi\sigma^{m}\bar{\sigma}^{n}\chi\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\psi\sigma^{m}\bar{\chi}

\displaystyle=-\bar{\chi}\bar{\sigma}^{m}\psi\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\theta\sigma^{m}\bar{\theta}\right)\left(\theta\sigma^{n}\bar{\theta}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\theta^{2}\bar{\theta}^{2}\eta^{{m}{n}}\ ,

\displaystyle\theta\sigma^{m}\bar{\sigma}^{n}\theta

\displaystyle=\theta^{2}\eta^{{m}{n}}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\theta\psi\right)\left(\theta\chi\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\theta^{2}\psi\chi\ ,

\displaystyle\psi\sigma^{m}\bar{\sigma}^{n}\chi

\displaystyle=\chi\sigma^{n}\bar{\sigma}^{m}\psi\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\bar{\theta}\bar{\psi}\right)\left(\bar{\theta}\bar{\chi}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\theta}^{2}\bar{\psi}\bar{\chi}\ .

A.1.2
Superspace algebra

In the superspace formalism one introduces auxiliary fermionic coordinates
\left(\theta_{\alpha},\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\alpha}}\right)
. These are grassmann,
i.e.
anticommuting coordinates, which are, by definition, nilpotent:

\theta_{1}^{2}=\theta_{2}^{2}=\bar{\theta}_{\dot{1}}^{2}=\bar{\theta}_{\dot{2}}^{2}=0\,\,\longleftrightarrow\,\,\theta^{3}=\bar{\theta}^{3}=0\ .

(A.1.9)

A function of this coordinates has therefore a finite expansion. Taking one such coordinate
\vartheta
as an example, one has

F(\vartheta)=F_{0}+F_{1}\vartheta\ .

(A.1.10)

Integration in such grassmann variable
\theta
follows the rules

\displaystyle\int d\vartheta=0\ ,

\displaystyle\int d\vartheta\,\vartheta=1\ ,

(A.1.11)

and

\displaystyle\int d\vartheta\,F(\vartheta)=F_{1}=\partial_{\vartheta}F(\vartheta)\ ,

\displaystyle\int d\vartheta\,\vartheta F(\vartheta)=F_{0}\ ,

\displaystyle\int d\vartheta\,\partial_{\vartheta}F(\vartheta)=0\ .

(A.1.12)

In the case of superspace,
i.e.
with four such grassmann coordinates, one has the integration measures

\displaystyle d^{2}\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}d\theta^{\alpha}d\theta^{\beta}=\frac{1}{2}d\theta^{1}d\theta^{2}\ ,

(A.1.13)

\displaystyle d^{2}\bar{\theta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}d\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\alpha}}d\bar{\theta}_{\dot{\beta}}=-\frac{1}{2}d\bar{\theta}_{1}d\bar{\theta}_{2}\ ,

\displaystyle d^{4}\theta

\displaystyle=d^{2}\theta d^{2}\bar{\theta}\ ,

and basic integration rules

\int\theta^{2}d^{2}\theta=\int\bar{\theta}^{2}d^{2}\bar{\theta}=1\ .

(A.1.14)

Superspace covariant derivatives have been defined in eq. (
1.2.7
) to be

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}=\partial_{\alpha}+i\left(\sigma^{m}\bar{\theta}\right)_{\alpha}\partial_{m}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{\dot{\alpha}}=-\bar{\partial}_{\dot{\alpha}}-i\left(\theta\sigma^{m}\right)_{\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{m}\ .

(A.1.15)

They also satisfy a set of useful identities, namely

\displaystyle D_{\alpha}D_{\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\alpha\beta}D^{2}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{\dot{\alpha}}\bar{D}_{\dot{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\beta}}\bar{D}^{2}\ ,

(A.1.16)

\displaystyle\{D_{\alpha},\bar{D}_{\dot{\alpha}}\}

\displaystyle=-2i\sigma_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}^{m}\partial_{m}\ ,

\displaystyle\{D_{\alpha},D_{\beta}\}

\displaystyle=\{\bar{D}_{\dot{\alpha}},\bar{D}_{\dot{\beta}}\}=0\ ,

\displaystyle D^{2}\theta^{2}

\displaystyle=-4+4i\theta\sigma^{m}\bar{\theta}\partial_{m}+\theta^{2}\bar{\theta}^{2}\square\ ,

\displaystyle\left[D_{\alpha},\bar{D}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=-4i\sigma_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}^{m}\bar{D}^{\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{m}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{D}^{2}\bar{\theta}^{2}

\displaystyle=-4-4i\theta\sigma^{m}\bar{\theta}\partial_{m}+\theta^{2}\bar{\theta}^{2}\square\ ,

\displaystyle\left[D^{2},\bar{D}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=-8iD^{\alpha}\sigma_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}^{m}\bar{D}^{\dot{\alpha}}\partial_{m}+16\square\ ,

A.1.3
Four-component spinors and Dirac matrices

Dirac four-component spinors are obtained by joining together two distinct Weyl fermions:

\Psi=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\alpha}\\
\bar{\chi}^{\dot{\alpha}}\end{pmatrix}\ .

(A.1.17)

The associated Dirac matrices
\gamma^{m}
satisfy the Clifford algebra relation

\left\{\gamma^{m},\gamma^{n}\right\}=2\eta^{mn}\ ,

(A.1.18)

so that their hermitian conjugates are such that

\displaystyle\left(\gamma^{\mu}\right)^{\dagger}=\gamma^{0}\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{0}

\displaystyle\longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\gamma^{0}\right)^{\dagger}=-\gamma^{0}\ ,\,\,\,\ \left(\gamma^{i}\right)^{\dagger}=\gamma^{i}\ ,

(A.1.19)

and Dirac conjugation is defined as

\bar{\Psi}=i\Psi^{\dagger}\gamma^{0}\ .

(A.1.20)

Throughout the manuscript, we make use of the following explicit representation:

\displaystyle\gamma^{m}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{0}&-i\sigma^{m}\\
-i\bar{\sigma}^{m}&\mathbb{0}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

\displaystyle\gamma_{5}=i\gamma^{0}\gamma^{1}\gamma^{2}\gamma^{3}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{1}&\mathbb{0}\\
\mathbb{0}&-\mathbb{1}\end{pmatrix}\ .

(A.1.21)

The
\gamma_{5}
matrix is hermitian,
i.e.

\gamma_{5}^{\dagger}=\gamma_{5}
, and allows to introduce the chiral projectors

\displaystyle P_{L}=\frac{1-\gamma_{5}}{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{0}&\mathbb{0}\\
\mathbb{0}&\mathbb{1}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

\displaystyle P_{R}=\frac{1+\gamma_{5}}{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{1}&\mathbb{0}\\
\mathbb{0}&\mathbb{0}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

(A.1.22)

so that

\displaystyle\Psi_{L,R}\equiv P_{L,R}\Psi\ ,

\displaystyle\Psi_{L}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\bar{\chi}^{\dot{\alpha}}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

\displaystyle\Psi_{R}=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\alpha}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\Psi}_{L,R}=\bar{\Psi}P_{L,R}=\overline{\Psi_{R,L}}\ .

(A.1.23)

The Clifford algebra (
A.1.18
) is spanned by the basis

\displaystyle\left\{\mathbb{1},\gamma^{{m}},\gamma^{{m}{n}},\gamma^{{m}}\gamma_{5},\gamma_{5}\right\}\ ,

(A.1.24)

whose elements satisfy the identity

\gamma^{m_{1}\dots m_{r}}=-\frac{i}{\left(4-r\right)!}\varepsilon^{m_{r}\dots m_{1}r_{1}\dots r_{4-r}}\gamma_{r_{1}\dots r_{4-r}}\gamma_{5}\ ,

(A.1.25)

where we employ the standard definition
\gamma^{{m}_{1}\dots{m}_{r}}\equiv\gamma^{[{m}_{1}}\dots\gamma^{{m}_{r}]}
. The associated charge conjugation matrix
C_{4}
is taken to be

\displaystyle C_{4}=\begin{pmatrix}\sigma^{2}&\mathbb{0}\\
\mathbb{0}&-\sigma^{2}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

\displaystyle C_{4}^{\textup{T}}=C_{4}^{\dagger}=C_{4}^{-1}=-C_{4}\ ,

(A.1.26)

and it defines the symmetry property of the Clifford algebra (
A.1.18
)

\displaystyle\left(C_{4}\gamma^{{m}_{1}\dots{m}_{r}}\right)^{\textup{T}}=-t_{r}C_{4}\gamma^{{m}_{1}\dots{m}_{r}}\ ,

(A.1.27)

With
t_{r}=1
for
r=0,3
and
t_{r}=-1
for
t=1,2
modulo 4. Moreover, a generic element
M
of the Clifford algebra can always be written as an expansion in the basis (
A.1.24
). This is the basic Fierz identity, given by

\displaystyle M=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{Tr}\left(M\right)\mathbb{1}+\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\gamma^{m}M\right)\gamma_{m}-\frac{1}{8}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\gamma^{mn}M\right)\gamma_{{m}{n}}

(A.1.28)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\gamma^{m}\gamma_{5}M\right)\gamma_{m}\gamma_{5}+\frac{1}{4}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\gamma_{5}M\right)\gamma_{5}\ .

The special Dirac fermion (
A.1.17
) which is made by hermitian conjugated components
\bar{\chi}^{\dot{\alpha}}\equiv\bar{\psi}^{\dot{\alpha}}
is called a Majorana fermion:

\lambda=\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{\alpha}\\
\bar{\psi}^{\dot{\alpha}}\end{pmatrix}\ .

(A.1.29)

From the four-component point of view, a Majorana fermion is defined by the reality condition

\bar{\lambda}=\lambda^{\textup{T}}C_{4}\ .

(A.1.30)

In curved spacetime, the above Clifford algebra is upgraded to

\displaystyle\left\{\gamma^{\mu},\gamma^{\nu}\right\}=2g^{\mu\nu}\ ,

\displaystyle\gamma^{\mu}=e^{\mu}{}_{m}\gamma^{m}\ .

(A.1.31)

A.2
Algebra of the helicity eigenstate decomposition

In this section we collect the detailed equations and properties that characterize the helicity eigenstate decomposition employed in Section
2.2
when discussing the dynamics of the gravitino in the FLRW spacetime. We recall that the flat Dirac matrices are denoted, in the context of this discussion, with Greek indices and a bar on top, following

\gamma^{\mu}=e^{\mu}{}_{a}\gamma^{a}=a(\tau)^{-1}\gamma^{a}\equiv a(\tau)^{-1}\bar{\gamma}^{\mu}\ .

(A.2.1)

The indices contraction rules and notation in momentum space are

\displaystyle\vec{k}\cdot\vec{x}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}k^{i}x^{i}=k^{i}x_{i}\ ,

\displaystyle\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\cdot\vec{\psi}_{k}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bar{\gamma}^{i}\psi_{i,k}=\bar{\gamma}^{i}\psi_{i,k}\ ,

(A.2.2)

\displaystyle\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\psi}_{k}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}k^{i}\psi_{i,k}=k^{i}\psi_{i,k}\ ,

\displaystyle\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}k^{i}\bar{\gamma}^{i}=k_{i}\bar{\gamma}^{i}\ ,

and the following identities hold:

\displaystyle\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)=\vec{k}^{2}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\bar{\gamma}^{i}\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)=2k^{i}\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)-\vec{k}^{2}\bar{\gamma}^{i}\ .

(A.2.3)

The projectors introduced in eq. (
2.2.22
) satisfy the following algebra:

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}^{i}\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i}=-1\ ,

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}^{i}\left(P_{k}\right)_{i}=0\ ,

(A.2.4)

\displaystyle k^{i}\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i}=0\ ,

\displaystyle k^{i}\left(P_{k}\right)_{i}=-1\ ,

\displaystyle\left\{\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i},\bar{\gamma}_{0}\right\}=0\ ,

\displaystyle\left\{\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i},\bar{\gamma}_{j}\right\}=-\eta_{ij}+\frac{k_{i}k_{j}}{\vec{k}^{2}}\ ,

\displaystyle\left[\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{k},\bar{\gamma}_{0}\right]=0\ ,

\displaystyle\left[\left(P_{k}\right)_{i},\bar{\gamma}_{j}\right]=\frac{1}{\vec{k}^{2}}\left[\bar{\gamma}_{i}k_{j}+\bar{\gamma}_{j}k_{i}+\eta_{ij}\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i}^{\dagger}=\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(P_{k}\right)_{i}^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{2\vec{k}^{2}}\left[\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\bar{\gamma}_{i}-3k_{i}\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\left(P_{\gamma}\right)^{i}\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i}=\frac{1}{2}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(P_{\gamma}\right)^{i}\left(P_{k}\right)_{i}=-\frac{1}{2\vec{k}^{2}}\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\left(P_{k}\right)^{i}\left(P_{k}\right)_{i}=\frac{1}{4\vec{k}{}^{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(P_{k}\right)^{i}\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i}=\frac{1}{2\vec{k}^{2}}\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\left(P_{k}\right)^{i\,\dagger}\left(P_{k}\right)_{i}=\frac{3}{4\vec{k}{}^{4}}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(P_{k}\right)^{i\,\dagger}\left(P_{\gamma}\right)_{i}=-\frac{1}{2\vec{k}^{2}}\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\ .

The operator
\hat{O}_{i}
introduced in eq. (
2.2.25
)-(
2.2.26
) satisfies instead the following identities:

\displaystyle\bar{\gamma}^{0}\hat{O}_{i}^{\dagger}\bar{\gamma}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\vec{k}^{2}}\left\{-k_{i}\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)+\frac{ia}{2}\left(m+H\bar{\gamma}^{0}\right)\left[\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\bar{\gamma}_{i}-3k_{i}\right]\right\}\ ,

(A.2.5)

\displaystyle\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)\hat{O}_{i}

\displaystyle=-\left\{k_{i}+\frac{ia}{2}\left[\frac{k_{i}}{\vec{k}^{2}}\left(\vec{k}\cdot\vec{\bar{\gamma}}\right)+\bar{\gamma}_{i}\right]\left(m-H\bar{\gamma}^{0}\right)\right\}\ .

(A.2.6)

Appendix B
Symmetry and conservation of the gravitino stress-energy tensor

In this Appendix we explicitly prove the on-shell symmetry and covariant conservation of the energy-momentum tensor of the massive gravitino, computed in eq. (
2.2.100
) of Chapter
2
. Starting from the covariant action for the massive Majorana spin-
\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}
field of eq. (
2.2.10
), The necessary tools are the associated equations of motion and constraints, respectively in eq. (
2.2.11
)-(
2.2.13
) and in eq. (
2.2.12
), and the Majorana reality condition (
A.1.30
), along with the Levi–Civita tensor contraction rule (
A.0.7
) and the Clifford algebra identity (
A.1.24
).

The variation of the action (
2.2.10
) with respect to the vierbein yields:
1
1

1

In eq. (
B.0.1
)), the equations of motion (
2.2.11
) have already been applied once.

\displaystyle T^{\mu\nu}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\sigma\mu}\left({\nabla}_{\sigma}\Psi^{\nu}-{\nabla}^{\nu}\Psi_{\sigma}\right)-\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\mu}\Psi^{\nu}

(B.0.1)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\nabla_{\rho}\left(\bar{\Psi}^{\rho}\gamma^{\mu}\Psi^{\nu}+\bar{\Psi}^{\rho}\gamma^{\nu}\Psi^{\mu}-\bar{\Psi}^{\mu}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi^{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{\nu}\left(\bar{\Psi}^{\mu}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi_{\rho}\right)-\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu\nu}\,\nabla_{\rho}\left(\bar{\Psi}_{\sigma}\gamma^{\sigma}\Psi^{\rho}\right)\ .

Making use of the equations mentioned above, one can prove that the antisymmetric part of this tensor is vanishing on-shell:

\displaystyle T^{[\mu\nu]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\sigma[\mu|}\left({\nabla}_{\sigma}\Psi^{|\nu]}-{\nabla}^{|\nu]}\Psi_{\sigma}\right)-\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu|}\Psi^{|\nu]}

(B.0.2)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{[\nu}\left(\bar{\Psi}^{\mu]}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi_{\rho}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\nabla_{\rho}\left(\bar{\Psi}^{[\mu|}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi^{|\nu]}\right)=

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu}-\bar{\Psi}^{[\mu}\right)\left[\cancel{\nabla}\psi^{\nu]}-\nabla^{\nu]}\left(\gamma^{\rho}\Psi_{\rho}\right)\right]-\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu|}\Psi^{|\nu]}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}^{\sigma}\gamma^{[\mu}\left(\nabla_{\sigma}\Psi^{\nu]}-\nabla^{\nu]}\Psi_{\sigma}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho}\nabla^{[\mu}\Psi^{\nu]}=

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu}\Psi^{\nu]}-\frac{3}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{[\rho}\nabla^{\nu}\Psi^{\mu]}

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu}\Psi^{\nu]}-\frac{3}{2}\delta^{\rho\nu\mu}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\alpha}\nabla^{\beta}\Psi^{\gamma}=

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu}\Psi^{\nu]}+\frac{i}{4}\varepsilon^{\rho\nu\mu}{}_{\sigma}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma_{5}\left[\gamma^{\sigma\lambda\tau}\nabla_{\lambda}\Psi_{\tau}\right]=

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu}\Psi^{\nu]}+\frac{m}{4}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\nu\mu}\gamma^{\sigma}\Psi_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu}\Psi^{\nu]}-\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho[\mu}\Psi^{\nu]}=0\ .

Thus, the tensor in eq. (
B.0.1
) is equal, on-shell, to its symmetric part
T^{\mu\nu}=T^{(\mu\nu)}
, which is the tensor given in eq. (
2.2.100
).

The proof of the covariant conservation goes along similar lines. Together with the equations listed at the beginning, one needs the following tensor identities:

\displaystyle R^{\mu}_{[\nu\rho\sigma]}=0\ ,

(B.0.3)

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\gamma^{\mu\nu\lambda}\gamma^{\rho\sigma}=4G^{\lambda}_{\tau}\gamma^{\tau}\ ,

\displaystyle\left[\nabla_{\mu},\nabla_{\nu}\right]\Psi^{\rho}=\frac{1}{4}R_{\mu\nu\lambda\tau}\gamma^{\lambda\tau}\Psi^{\rho}+R^{\rho}{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}\Psi^{\sigma}\ ,

\displaystyle\left[\nabla_{\mu},\nabla_{\nu}\right]V^{\rho_{1}\dots\rho_{r}}=R^{\rho_{1}}{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}V^{\sigma\rho_{2}\dots\rho_{r}}+\dots+R^{\rho_{r}}{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}V^{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}\dots\sigma}\ .

We study the covariant divergence
\nabla_{\mu}T^{\mu\nu}
piece by piece. We do so working with the original tensor (
B.0.1
) rather than the symmetric one: the two are in fact equal, but the former is simpler to deal with. We start from the first line of the tensor (
B.0.1
), for which we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\mu}

\displaystyle\left[\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\sigma\mu}\left({\nabla}_{\sigma}\Psi^{\nu}-{\nabla}^{\nu}\Psi_{\sigma}\right)-m\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\mu}\Psi^{\nu}\right]=

(B.0.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\overset{\leftarrow}{\nabla}_{\mu}\gamma^{\rho\sigma\mu}\left(\nabla_{\sigma}\Psi^{\nu}-\nabla^{\nu}\Psi_{\sigma}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\sigma\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\sigma}\Psi^{\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\mu\sigma}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}\Psi_{\sigma}

\displaystyle-\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\overset{\leftarrow}{\nabla}_{\mu}\gamma^{\rho\mu}\Psi^{\nu}-\frac{m}{2}\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\mu}\nabla_{\mu}\Psi^{\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\mu}m\right)\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho\mu}\Psi^{\nu}=

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}R^{\nu}_{\,\rho\alpha\beta}\bar{\Psi}^{\alpha}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi^{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}R^{\nu}{}_{\rho}\bar{\Psi}_{\alpha}\gamma^{\alpha}\Psi^{\rho}\ .

Next, all the remaining pieces are total derivatives of gravitinos bilinear. From the point of view of general diffeomorphisms, they transform as tensors of the type
V^{\mu\nu\rho}
of eq. (
B.0.3
), so that there is no effective dependence on the Lorentz spin-connection in the covariant derivatives. Thus, employing the notation
V^{\mu\nu\rho}\equiv\bar{\Psi}^{\mu}\gamma^{\nu}\Psi^{\rho}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bullet\quad-\frac{1}{4}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\rho}&\left[\bar{\Psi}^{\rho}\gamma^{\nu}\Psi^{\mu}\right]=-\frac{i}{8}\left[\nabla_{\mu},\nabla_{\rho}\right]V^{\rho\nu\mu}=\\
=&-\frac{1}{8}\left(R^{\rho}_{\sigma\mu\rho}V^{\sigma\nu\mu}+R^{\mu}_{\sigma\mu\rho}V^{\rho\nu\sigma}+R^{\nu}_{\sigma\mu\rho}V^{\rho\sigma\mu}\right)=\frac{1}{8}R^{\nu}_{\rho\alpha\beta}\bar{\Psi}^{\alpha}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi^{\beta}\ ,\end{aligned}

(B.0.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bullet\quad-\frac{1}{4}\nabla_{\mu}\nabla_{\rho}&\left[\bar{\Psi}^{\rho}\gamma^{\mu}\Psi^{\nu}-\bar{\Psi}^{\mu}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi^{\nu}\right]=-\frac{1}{4}\left[\nabla_{\mu},\nabla_{\rho}\right]V^{\rho\mu\nu}=\\
=&-\frac{1}{4}\left(R^{\rho}_{\sigma\mu\rho}V^{\sigma\mu\nu}+R^{\mu}_{\sigma\mu\rho}V^{\rho\sigma\nu}+R^{\nu}_{\sigma\mu\rho}V^{\rho\mu\sigma}\right)=-\frac{1}{4}R^{\nu}_{\beta\rho\alpha}\bar{\Psi}^{\alpha}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi^{\beta}\ ,\end{aligned}

(B.0.6)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bullet\quad-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\mu}&\left[g^{\mu\nu}\nabla_{\rho}\left(\bar{\Psi}_{\alpha}\gamma^{\alpha}\Psi^{\rho}\right)+\nabla^{\nu}\left(\bar{\Psi}^{\mu}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi_{\rho}\right)\right]=\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\left[\nabla^{\nu},\nabla_{\mu}\right]\left(\bar{\Psi}_{\rho}\gamma^{\rho}\Psi^{\mu}\right)V^{\mu}=\frac{1}{2}R^{\nu}{}_{\rho}\bar{\Psi}_{\alpha}\gamma^{\alpha}\Psi^{\rho}\ .\end{aligned}

(B.0.7)

Therefore, summing up all the terms in eq. (
B.0.4
))–(
B.0.7
) and applying the Bianchi identity of the Riemann tensor in eq. (
B.0.3
), we find that the energy-momentum tensor (
2.2.100
) is indeed covariantly conserved:

\nabla_{\mu}T^{\mu\nu}=0\ .

(B.0.8)

Appendix C
The Kaluza–Klein reduction of the 5D supergravity algebra on
{S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}}

This appendix is devoted to the detailed Kaluza–Klein reduction of the 5D pure supergravity algebra on the orbifold
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, from which it is possible to extract the
D=4
,
\mathcal{N}=1
massive spin-2 supermultiplet of eq. (
2.4.4
). The field content of 5D pure supergravity is given by the fünfbein
E_{M}{}^{A}
, one abelian gauge field
\mathcal{A}_{M}
and one
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{R}
-Majorana gravitino
\Psi_{M}
, which is actually a doublet of the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{R}

R
-symmetry of the theory. This type of fermions is associated with the so-called symplectic Clifford algebra, described by the following five-dimensional symplectic Dirac matrices:

\displaystyle\gamma^{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{1}\otimes\left(i\sigma_{1}\otimes\mathbb{1}\right),\qquad\gamma^{i}=\mathbb{1}\otimes\left(\sigma_{2}\otimes\sigma^{i}\right),\qquad\gamma^{4}=\mathbb{1}\otimes\left(\sigma_{3}\otimes\mathbb{1}\right)\ ,

(C.0.1)

where the elements inside the parenthesis correspond to the usual Dirac indices, whereas the elements in front correspond to the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{R}
indices. In this setup, the appropriate charge conjugation matrix
\mathcal{C}_{5}
is given by

\mathcal{C}_{5}=\sigma_{2}\otimes\left(\mathbb{1}\otimes\sigma_{2}\right)\equiv\sigma_{2}\otimes\left(\gamma_{5}C_{4}\right)\ ,

(C.0.2)

and it is such that

\mathcal{C}_{5}^{-1}=\mathcal{C}_{5}^{\textup{T}}=\mathcal{C}_{5}^{\dagger}=\mathcal{C}_{5}\ .

(C.0.3)

By construction, this symplectic Clifford algebra possess the symmetry property

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{C}_{5}\gamma^{M_{1}\dots M_{r}}\right)^{\textup{T}}=

\displaystyle-t_{r}\mathcal{C}_{5}\gamma^{M_{1}\dots M_{r}}\ ,

(C.0.4)

with
t_{r}=-1
for
r=0,1\,[4]
and
t_{r}=1
for
r=2,3\,[4]
. Thus, a five-dimensional, symplectic-Majorana fermion
\lambda
is defined by the condition

\bar{\lambda}=\lambda^{\textup{T}}\mathcal{C}_{5}\ .

(C.0.5)

In this section we will work directly with symplectic-Majorana spinors like (
C.0.5
) as long as five-dimensional quantities are concerned. We will then open the
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{R}
components, which we denote with
\lambda^{i}
, with
i=1,2
, when needed, in particular when performing the dimensional reduction on the orbifold
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

We can now go back to the discussion of such a compactification, which we begin from the supersymmetry transformations of pure 5D supergravity. At the linearized level, they are
[
171
,
172
]

\displaystyle\delta E^{A}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\varepsilon}\gamma^{A}\Psi,

(C.0.6)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{A}=-\frac{i}{2}\bar{\varepsilon}\Psi,

\displaystyle\delta\Psi=\mathcal{D}\varepsilon+iE^{A}\left(\frac{1}{8}\gamma_{A}{}^{BC}\mathcal{F}_{BC}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}_{AB}\gamma^{B}\right)\varepsilon,

where
\mathcal{F}_{MN}=2\partial_{[M}\mathcal{A}_{N]}
is the abelian gauge field strength,
\mathcal{D}_{M}
is the five-dimensional Lorentz-covariant derivative and
\varepsilon
is the 5D supersymmetry parameter. To perform the desired dimensional reduction, we start from the compactification ansatz

\displaystyle E^{a}=\phi^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{a},

(C.0.7)

\displaystyle E^{4}=\phi\left(dy+B\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=a\left(dy+B\right)+A,

\displaystyle\Psi=\phi^{\frac{5}{4}}\,\zeta\left(dy+B\right)+\phi^{-\frac{1}{4}}\left(\psi-\frac{1}{2}e^{a}\gamma_{a}\gamma_{5}\zeta\right),

in which
y
is the compact dimension and
e^{a}
is the vierbein,
A
and
B
are 4D abelian gauge fields, of field strengths
F=dA
and
G=dB
,
a
and
\phi
are two real scalar fields, and
\psi
and
\zeta
are two
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{R}
-Majorana fermions. Alongside this ansatz, it is convenient to redefine also the five dimensional supersymmetry parameter
\varepsilon
as

\varepsilon=\phi^{-\frac{1}{4}}\epsilon.

(C.0.8)

with
\epsilon
that will give the supersymmetry parameter in four dimensions.

The next step of this procedure is to plug the compactification ansatz (
C.0.7
) into the 5D algebra (
C.0.6
), which yields its decomposition in terms of the 4D parameterization of (
C.0.7
). In order to do this, we need the 4D decomposition along the ansatz (
C.0.7
) of the components of the 5D gauge field strength
\mathcal{F}_{MN}
and spin-connection
\left(\omega_{M}\right)^{A}{}_{B}
. The former is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{ab}=

\displaystyle\phi e_{a}{}^{\mu}e_{b}{}^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{a4}=

\displaystyle\phi^{-\frac{1}{2}}e_{a}{}^{\mu}\left(\partial_{\mu}a-\partial_{y}A_{\mu}\right).

(C.0.9)

The latter is more conveniently expressed through the anholonomy coefficients
\Omega_{AB}{}^{C}
, according to

\displaystyle dE^{A}=\frac{1}{2}\Omega_{BC}{}^{A}{}E^{B}\wedge E^{C},

\displaystyle\left(\omega_{C}\right)_{AB}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\Omega_{CAB}-\Omega_{CBA}+\Omega_{BAC}\right),

(C.0.10)

which decompose as

\displaystyle\Omega_{bc}{}^{a}=

\displaystyle\phi^{\frac{1}{2}}\Omega^{(4)}_{bc}{}^{a}-\phi^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\mu}\phi e_{[b}{}^{\mu}\delta^{a}_{c]},

\displaystyle\Omega_{ab}{}^{4}=

\displaystyle\phi^{2}\,e_{[a}{}^{\mu}e_{b]}{}^{\nu}G_{\mu\nu},

(C.0.11)

\displaystyle\Omega_{b4}{}^{a}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\phi^{-2}\partial_{y}\phi\,\delta^{a}_{b}-\phi^{-1}e_{b}{}^{\mu}\partial_{y}e_{\mu}{}^{a},

\displaystyle\Omega_{a4}{}^{4}=

\displaystyle\phi^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\,e_{a}{}^{\mu}\left(\partial_{\mu}\phi-\phi\partial_{y}B_{\mu}\right).

where
\Omega^{(4)}_{bc}
are the anholonomy coefficients in 4D. Moreover, consistency between the compactification ansatz (
C.0.7
) and the structure of the original 5D supergravity algebra (
C.0.6
) requires, when performing the reduction, to implement the latter with an additional 5D local Lorentz transformation. At the linearised level, such transformations affects only the fünfbein, on which it acts as

\delta_{\textup{LL}}E^{A}=\Lambda^{A}{}_{B}E^{B},

(C.0.12)

and the explicit transformation required is given by

\displaystyle\Lambda^{a}{}_{b}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\phi^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\bar{\varepsilon}\gamma^{a}{}_{b}\gamma_{5}\zeta\right),

\displaystyle\Lambda^{a}{}_{4}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\phi^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\bar{\varepsilon}\gamma^{a}\zeta\right)

(C.0.13)

The 4D supersymmetry transformations resulting from this procedure are then

\displaystyle\delta e_{\mu}{}^{a}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma^{a}\psi_{\mu},

(C.0.14)

\displaystyle\delta\phi=\frac{\phi}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma_{5}\zeta,

\displaystyle\delta a=-\frac{i}{2}\phi\bar{\epsilon}\zeta,

\displaystyle\delta A_{\mu}=-\frac{i}{2}\phi^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\epsilon}\left(\psi_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\zeta\right),

\displaystyle\delta B_{\mu}=\frac{\phi^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\left(\gamma_{5}\psi_{\mu}+\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta\right),

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta\zeta=&-\frac{\phi^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{4}\partial_{y}\phi\epsilon+\frac{\phi^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{4}Q_{ab}\gamma^{ab}\epsilon+\frac{i}{8}\phi^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(F_{\rho\sigma}+i\phi G_{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\right)\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\epsilon\\
&+\frac{i}{2}\phi^{-1}\left[\partial_{\rho}a-\partial_{y}A_{\rho}+i\left(\phi\partial_{y}B_{\rho}-\partial_{\rho}\phi\right)\gamma_{5}\right]\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon,\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta\psi_{\mu}=&D_{\mu}\epsilon-\frac{3}{8}\phi^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial_{y}\phi\gamma_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\epsilon+\frac{\phi^{-\frac{3}{2}}}{2}e^{a}_{\mu}\left(P_{ab}\gamma^{b}+\frac{1}{4}Q_{bc}\gamma_{a}\gamma^{bc}\right)\gamma_{5}\epsilon\\
&-\frac{1}{4}\partial_{y}B_{\rho}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon+\frac{3i}{4}\phi^{-1}\left(\partial_{y}A_{\mu}-\partial_{\mu}a\right)\gamma_{5}\epsilon-\frac{i}{8}\phi^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(3F_{\mu\rho}^{+,5}-i\phi G_{\mu\rho}^{+,5}\gamma_{5}\right)\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon.\end{aligned}

In these formulae,
D_{\mu}
is the 4D Lorentz-covariant derivative, while
\epsilon
is the 4D supersymmetry parameter defined in (
C.0.8
). The quantities
P_{ab}
and
Q_{ab}
are defined to be

\displaystyle P_{ab}\equiv e_{(a}{}^{\lambda}\partial_{y}e_{b)}{}_{\lambda},

\displaystyle Q_{ab}\equiv e_{[a}{}^{\lambda}\partial_{y}e_{b]}{}_{\lambda},

(C.0.15)

while
F_{\mu\nu}^{\pm,5}
and
G_{\mu\nu}^{\pm,5}
are the self-dual field strengths dressed by a
\gamma_{5}
, defined as

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}^{\pm,5}\equiv F_{\mu\nu}\pm i\tilde{F}_{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5},

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{\mu\rho}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}F_{\rho\sigma}.

(C.0.16)

The transformations (
C.0.14
) still carry the full dependence on the
5^{\textup{th}}
compact coordinate
y
. Thus, the next step is to properly expand each field in modes along the
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
interval spanned by
y
, which will further decompose the transformations (
C.0.14
) into massless and massive transformation rules, properly organized in
\mathcal{N}=1
multiplets. This mode expansion has to be consistent with the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
parity along
y
modded out by the orbifold projection under consideration, namely with the transformation of each field under such
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, fixed by the invariance of the lagrangian
1
1

1

For the explicit expression of the lagrangian of pure 5D supergravity, see
[
171
,
172
]
. Starting from the bosons, the vierbein
e_{\mu}{}^{a}
and the scalars
\phi
and
a
are even under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, while the gauge vectors
A_{\mu}
and
B_{\mu}
are odd: this fixes their Kaluza--Klein expansion to be
2
2

2

The expansion of the field
\phi
is fixed to the one in (
C.0.17
) by the additional requirement of being an exponential function.

\displaystyle e_{\mu}{}^{a}(y)=

\displaystyle e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\mu}{}^{a}(x)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{\mu}{}^{a}(x)\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right),

\displaystyle B_{\mu}(y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}B_{\mu}^{(n)}(x)\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right),

(C.0.17)

\displaystyle\phi(y)=

\displaystyle\phi^{(0)}(x)\left[1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\phi^{(n)}(x)\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)\right],

\displaystyle A_{\mu}(y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}A_{\mu}^{(n)}(x)\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right),

\displaystyle a(y)=

\displaystyle a^{(0)}(x)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a^{(n)}(x)\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right).

The projection on the fermions is instead more involved. The action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is in fact chiral and breaks therefore the original
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{R}
symmetry:

\displaystyle\Psi_{i}{}_{\mu}(-y)=\sigma^{3}_{ij}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{j}{}_{\mu}(y),

\displaystyle\Psi_{i}{}_{5}(-y)=-\sigma^{3}_{ij}\gamma_{5}\Psi_{j}{}_{5}(y),

(C.0.18)

so that

\displaystyle\psi_{1}{}_{\mu}(-y)=

\displaystyle\gamma_{5}\psi_{1}{}_{\mu}(y),

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}{}_{\mu}(-y)=

\displaystyle-\gamma_{5}\zeta_{1}{}_{\mu}(y),

(C.0.19)

\displaystyle\psi_{2}{}_{\mu}(-y)=

\displaystyle-\gamma_{5}\psi_{2}{}_{\mu}(y),

\displaystyle\zeta_{2}{}_{\mu}(-y)=

\displaystyle\gamma_{5}\zeta_{2}{}_{\mu}(y).

Then, the Kaluza–Klein mode expansion for the fermions result to be

\displaystyle\psi_{1\mu}=

\displaystyle\psi_{1\mu\text{R}}+\psi_{1\mu\text{L}}=\psi^{(0)}_{1\mu\text{R}}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{R}}\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)+i\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{L}}\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)\right],

(C.0.20)

\displaystyle\psi_{2\mu}=

\displaystyle\psi_{1\mu\text{L}}+\psi_{1\mu\text{R}}=\psi^{(0)}_{2\mu\text{L}}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{L}}\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)+i\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{R}}\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}=

\displaystyle\zeta_{1\text{L}}+\zeta_{1\text{R}}=\zeta^{(0)}_{1\text{L}}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{L}}\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)+i\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{R}}\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\zeta_{2}=

\displaystyle\zeta_{2\text{R}}+\zeta_{2\text{L}}=\zeta^{(0)}_{2\text{R}}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{R}}\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)+i\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{L}}\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right)\right].

This decomposition is consistent with the original symplectic Majorana condition, which at the level of the modes fixes, for every
n\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{R}}=-\left(\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{L}}\right)^{*},

\displaystyle\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{L}}=-\left(\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{R}}\right)^{*},

\displaystyle\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{R}}=\left(\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{L}}\right)^{*},

\displaystyle\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{L}}=\left(\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{R}}\right)^{*}.

(C.0.21)

Also the (local) supersymmetry parameter undergoes an analogous Kaluza–Klein decomposition and the associated zero mode correspond to the actual 4D supersymmetry parameters. The action of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry is

\epsilon_{i}(-y)=\sigma^{3}_{ij}\gamma_{5}\epsilon_{j}(y),

(C.0.22)

where
\sigma^{3}
is the third Pauli matrix. The actual 4D supersymmetry parameters correspond to the zero modes of
\epsilon_{i}(y)
(the massive ones are discarded), which the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
parity (
C.0.22
) constrains to be

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{1\text{R}},

\displaystyle\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon_{2\text{L}},

(C.0.23)

with
\epsilon_{2\text{L}}=\left(\epsilon_{1\text{R}}\right)^{*}
, consistently with the five dimensional symplectic-Majorana condition. This indicates therefore that the combination

\epsilon\equiv\epsilon_{1\text{R}}+\epsilon_{2\text{L}},

(C.0.24)

that defines a 4D Majorana fermion, is the actual supersymmetry parameter in four dimension, showing explicitly how the orbifold projection breaks the original
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetry in 5D to
\mathcal{N}=1
in 4D.

Plugging the expansions (
C.0.17
) and (
C.0.20
) into (
C.0.14
) yields the transformation rules associated to every Kaluza–Klein mode of the various fields, both massless and massive, which we now discuss separately.

C.1
The massless sector

The supersymmetry transformations of the massless modes result to be

\displaystyle\delta e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\mu}{}^{a}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\gamma^{a}\psi^{(0)}_{1\mu}+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\gamma^{a}\psi^{(0)}_{2\mu}\right),

(C.1.1)

\displaystyle\delta\phi^{(0)}=\frac{\phi^{(0)}}{2}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\zeta^{(0)}_{2}-\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\zeta^{(0)}_{1}\right),

\displaystyle\delta a^{(0)}=-\frac{i}{2}\phi^{(0)}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\zeta^{(0)}_{1}+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\zeta^{(0)}_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\delta\psi^{(0)}_{1\mu R}=D_{\mu}\epsilon_{1}-\frac{3i}{4}{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}\partial_{\mu}a^{(0)}\epsilon_{1},

\displaystyle\delta\psi^{(0)}_{2\mu L}=D_{\mu}\epsilon_{2}+\frac{3i}{4}{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}\partial_{\mu}a^{(0)}\epsilon_{2},

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{(0)}_{2R}=\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}}{2}\partial_{\rho}\left(\phi^{(0)}+ia^{(0)}\right)\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{2},

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{(0)}_{1L}=-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}}{2}\partial_{\rho}\left(\phi^{(0)}-ia^{(0)}\right)\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{1},

and they correspond to pure
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity coupled to a chiral multiplet. This structure becomes manifest once the fields are redefined in a proper way. Equations (
C.0.21
) and (
C.0.24
) suggest to recombine the Majorana massless fermions as

\displaystyle\psi_{\mu}\equiv\psi^{(0)}_{1\mu R}+\psi^{(0)}_{2\mu L},

\displaystyle\zeta\equiv\phi^{(0)}\left(\zeta^{(0)}_{2R}-\zeta^{(0)}_{1L}\right).

(C.1.2)

These redefinitions, together with

T\equiv\phi^{(0)}+ia^{(0)},

(C.1.3)

and the renaming
e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\mu}{}^{a}\to e_{\mu}{}^{a}
, fix the transformations of the massless sector to be

\displaystyle\delta e_{\mu}{}^{a}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\epsilon}\gamma^{a}\psi_{\mu},

(C.1.4)

\displaystyle\delta\psi_{\mu}=D_{\mu}\epsilon+\frac{i}{2}Q_{\mu}\gamma_{5}\epsilon,

\displaystyle\delta T=\bar{\epsilon}_{\text{R}}\zeta_{\text{R}},

\displaystyle\delta\zeta_{\text{R}}=\frac{1}{2}\cancel{\partial}T\epsilon_{\text{L}},

which are the transformations of pure
D=4
,
\mathcal{N}=1
supergravity
\left\{e_{\mu}{}^{a},\psi_{\mu}\right\}
coupled to chiral multiplet
\left\{T,\zeta\right\}

[
39
,
40
]
, with Kähler potential and associated Kähler-covariantisation given by

\displaystyle K=

\displaystyle-3\log\left(T+\overline{T}\right),

\displaystyle Q_{\mu}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2i\left(T+\overline{T}\right)}\left(\partial_{\mu}T-\partial_{\mu}\overline{T}\right).

(C.1.5)

This is a no-scale Kähler potential expected in Kaluza–Klein reductions of this type.

C.2
The massive sector

We then move to the massive sector. We define the Kaluza–Klein mass of every mode as

\displaystyle m_{n}={\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\frac{n}{R},

(C.2.1)

with
R
being the length of the compact dimension. Using then the formulae

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\phi=

\displaystyle-{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{5}{2}}\sum_{n}m_{n}\phi^{(n)}\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right),

\displaystyle P_{ab}=

\displaystyle-{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{n}m_{n}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{(a}{}^{\rho}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{b)}{}_{\rho}\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right),

(C.2.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{y}B_{\mu}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{n}m_{n}B^{(n)}_{\mu}\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right),

\displaystyle Q_{ab}=

\displaystyle-{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{n}m_{n}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{[a}{}^{\rho}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{b]}{}_{\rho}\sin\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{y}A_{\mu}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\sum_{n}m_{n}A^{(n)}_{\mu}\cos\left(\frac{ny}{R}\right),

\displaystyle\omega^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{\mu}{}^{ab}=

\displaystyle 2e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}{}^{[a|}{}^{\alpha}\partial^{\scalebox{0.6}{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}$(n)$}}_{[\mu}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{\alpha]}{}^{|b]}-e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\alpha}{}^{[a}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\beta}{}^{b]}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\mu}{}^{c}\partial^{\alpha}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{c}{}^{\beta},

one obtains that the massive modes of the set of supersymmetry transformations (
C.0.14
). The bosonic transformation rules are

\displaystyle\delta e_{n}{}^{a}_{\mu}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\gamma^{a}\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu}+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\gamma^{a}\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu}\right),

(C.2.3)

\displaystyle\delta\phi^{(n)}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\zeta^{(n)}_{2}-\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\zeta^{(n)}_{1}\right),

\displaystyle\delta a^{(n)}=-\frac{i}{2}\phi^{(0)}\left(\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\zeta^{(n)}_{1}+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\zeta^{(n)}_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\delta A^{(n)}_{\mu}=\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{2}\left[\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\left(\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{1}\right)+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\left(\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{2}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\delta B^{(n)}_{\mu}=\frac{i}{2}{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left[\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\left(\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu}+\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{2}\right)-\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\left(\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu}-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{1}\right)\right],

while the transformations on the massive spin-
\textstyle{\frac{1}{2}}
and spin-
\textstyle{\frac{3}{2}}
are respectively

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{L}}=\frac{m_{n}}{2}\left[-i\left({\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A^{(n)}_{\rho}-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}}{m_{n}}\partial_{\rho}a^{(n)}\right)+\left({\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}B^{(n)}_{\rho}-\frac{1}{m_{n}}\partial_{\rho}\phi^{(n)}\right)\right]\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{1},

(C.2.4)

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{R}}=\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{8}\left(F^{(n)}_{\rho\sigma}+i\phi^{(0)}G^{(n)}_{\rho\sigma}\right)\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\epsilon_{1}+\frac{i}{4}m_{n}\left(e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{a}{}^{\rho}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{b}{}_{\rho}\gamma^{ab}-\phi^{(n)}\right)\epsilon_{1},

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{L}}=-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{8}\left(F^{(n)}_{\rho\sigma}-i\phi^{(0)}G^{(n)}_{\rho\sigma}\right)\gamma^{\rho\sigma}\epsilon_{2}+\frac{i}{4}m_{n}\left(e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{a}{}^{\rho}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{b}{}_{\rho}\gamma^{ab}-\phi^{(n)}\right)\epsilon_{2},

\displaystyle\delta\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{R}}=\frac{m_{n}}{2}\left[-i\left({\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A^{(n)}_{\rho}-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}}{m_{n}}\partial_{\rho}a^{(n)}\right)-\left({\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}B^{(n)}_{\rho}-\frac{1}{m_{n}}\partial_{\rho}\phi^{(n)}\right)\right]\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{2},

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{R}}=&\frac{1}{4}\left(2e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}{}^{a}{}^{\alpha}\partial^{\scalebox{0.6}{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}$(n)$}}_{[\mu}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{\alpha]}{}^{b}-e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\alpha}{}^{a}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\beta}{}^{b}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\mu}{}^{c}\partial^{\alpha}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{c}{}^{\beta}\right)\gamma_{ab}\epsilon_{1}-\frac{m_{n}}{4}{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}B^{(n)}_{\rho}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{1}\\
&+\frac{3i}{4}m_{n}\left({\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A^{(n)}_{\mu}-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}a^{(n)}\right)\epsilon_{1},\end{aligned}

(C.2.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{L}}=&-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{8}\left(3F^{(n)+,5}_{\mu\rho}+i\phi^{(0)}G^{(n)+,5}_{\mu\rho}\right)\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{1}+\frac{i}{4}m_{n}g^{(n)}_{\mu\rho}\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{1}\\
&+\frac{i}{4}m_{n}\left(e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{a}{}^{\rho}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{b}{}_{\rho}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma^{ab}-\phi^{(n)}\gamma_{\mu}\right)\epsilon_{1},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{R}}=&-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{8}\left(3F^{(n)+,5}_{\mu\rho}-i\phi^{(0)}G^{(n)+,5}_{\mu\rho}\right)\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{2}-\frac{i}{4}m_{n}g^{(n)}_{\mu\rho}\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{2}\\
&-\frac{i}{4}m_{n}\left(e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{a}{}^{\rho}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{b}{}_{\rho}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma^{ab}-\phi^{(n)}\gamma_{\mu}\right)\epsilon_{2},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{L}}=&\frac{1}{4}\left(2e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}{}^{a}{}^{\alpha}\partial^{\scalebox{0.6}{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}$(n)$}}_{[\mu}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{\alpha]}{}^{b}-e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\alpha}{}^{a}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\beta}{}^{b}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{\mu}{}^{c}\partial^{\alpha}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{c}{}^{\beta}\right)\gamma_{ab}\epsilon_{2}-\frac{m_{n}}{4}{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}B^{(n)}_{\rho}\gamma_{\mu}\gamma^{\rho}\epsilon_{2}\\
&-\frac{3i}{4}m_{n}\left({\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A^{(n)}_{\mu}-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}a^{(n)}\right)\epsilon_{2}.\end{aligned}

The whole set massive degrees of freedom recombines, for each mode
n
, to a massive spin-1, two massive Majorana spin-
\textstyle{\frac{3}{2}}
and a massive spin-2 fields, according to the Stückelberg parameterisation, as customary for massive gauge fields. The Stückelberg symmetry comes naturally in dimensional compactifications, as the massive modes of the higher-dimensional gauge transformations
3
3

3

See
[
152
]
for an explicit analysis of this property in the case of the massive spin-2 field.
. To determine the combinations of the massive degrees of freedom of eqs. (
C.0.17
) and (
C.0.20
) associated to the unitary gauge of the correspondent Stückelberg symmetry, we start from the simplest (and more standard) of such combinations, the one for the massive spin-1 field, which we take to be

{A_{\mu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}={\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}A^{(n)}_{\mu}-\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{-1}}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}a^{(n)},

(C.2.6)

as suggested also by the massive spin-
\textstyle{\frac{3}{2}}
supersymmetry transformations (
C.2.5
). Acting with a supersymmetry transformation (
C.2.3
), one finds

\delta{A_{\mu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\bar{\epsilon}_{1}\left(\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{L}}-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{R}}+\frac{i}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{L}}\right)+\bar{\epsilon}_{2}\left(\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{R}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{L}}+\frac{i}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{R}}\right)\right],

(C.2.7)

which indicates that the correct Stückelberg combinations for
\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{L}}
and
\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{R}}
are

\displaystyle{{\psi_{1\mu\text{L}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}}{}^{\textup{S}}=

\displaystyle\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{L}}-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{R}}+\frac{i}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{L}},

(C.2.8)

\displaystyle{{\psi_{2\mu\text{R}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}}{}^{\textup{S}}=

\displaystyle\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{R}}+\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{L}}+\frac{i}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{R}},

where
{{\psi_{2\mu\text{R}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}}{}^{\textup{S}}=\left({{\psi_{1\mu\text{L}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}}{}^{\textup{S}}\right)^{*}
consistently, so that they can be combined in a Majorana spin-
\textstyle{\frac{3}{2}}
field

\displaystyle{\lambda_{\mu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}\equiv

\displaystyle{{\displaystyle\psi_{1\mu\text{L}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}}{}^{\textup{S}}+{{\psi_{2\mu\text{R}}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}}{}^{\textup{S}}=\left(\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{L}}+\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{R}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\left(\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{R}}-\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{L}}\right)+\frac{i}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}\left(\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{L}}+\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{R}}\right),

(C.2.9)

Proceeding in this way, one finds the correct Stückelberg combinations for the remaining spin-
\textstyle{\frac{3}{2}}
and spin-2 fields
{\chi_{\mu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}
and
{g_{\mu\nu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}
, which are

\displaystyle{\chi_{\mu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}=-i\left[\left(\psi^{(n)}_{1\mu\text{R}}-\psi^{(n)}_{2\mu\text{L}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\mu}\left(\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{L}}+\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{R}}\right)+\frac{i}{m_{n}}\partial_{\mu}\left(\zeta^{(n)}_{1\text{R}}-\zeta^{(n)}_{2\text{L}}\right)\right],

(C.2.10)

\displaystyle{g_{\mu\nu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}=2e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}_{(\mu}{}^{a}e^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}_{\nu)}{}_{a}-\phi^{(n)}g^{(0)}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{{\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(0)$}}}^{\frac{3}{2}}}{m_{n}}\left(\partial_{\mu}B^{(n)}_{\nu}+\partial_{\nu}B^{(n)}_{\mu}\right)-\frac{2}{m_{n}^{2}}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\phi^{(n)}.

(C.2.11)

which together with
{A_{\mu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}
and
{\lambda_{\mu}^{\scalebox{0.6}{$(n)$}}}{}^{\textup{S}}
form the massive spin-2 supermultiplet we were looking for. The linearised supersymmetry transformations of the above combinations, where one retains only the coupling to the massless modes of the vierbein
e_{\mu}{}^{a}
and the scalar
\phi
(but not their derivatives) and discards all other couplings, give us the algebra of a rigid massive spin-2 supermultiplet of mass
m_{n}
, for each
n
mode of the Kaluza–Klein tower. These are the transformations given in eq. (
2.4.4
), which can be thought of independently from the Kaluza–Klein reduction used to obtain them.

Appendix D
D-branes in orientifold constructions

.

In this final appendix, we complete the analysis of the brane spectra in the orientifold models discussed in Chapter
3
. We begin by examining the unstable lower-dimensional branes in the Dabholkar–Park orientifold introduced in Section
3.3
. We then turn to the new Scherk–Schwarz orientifold proposed in
[
3
]
and discuss those branes which were omitted from the analysis in Section
3.4
.

D.1
The unstable branes of the Dabholkar–Park orientifold

The unstable D-branes in the Dabholkar–Park orientifold are non-BPS D2, D3, D6 and D7-branes wrapping the compact circle, and all even-dimensional branes orthogonal to the circle. We now present all of them, referring to the discussion of Section
3.3
.

D.1.1
Non-BPS D7-branes wrapping the circle

The cylinder and Möbius amplitudes in the transverse channel for the wrapping D7-branes are equal to

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}_{77}=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-4}v}{2}\frac{1}{\eta^{8}}\left[\left(N+{\overline{N}}\right)^{2}V_{8}+\left(N-{\overline{N}}\right)^{2}S_{8}\right]\sum_{n}W_{n}\ ,

(D.1.1)

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{7}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2\epsilon v}{\hat{\eta}^{5}\hat{\vartheta}_{2}}\right)\left[\left(N+{\overline{N}}\right)\left({\hat{O}}_{6}{\hat{V}}_{2}-{\hat{V}}_{6}{\hat{O}}_{2}\right)-\left(N-{\overline{N}}\right)\left({\hat{S}}_{6}{\hat{S}}_{2}-{\hat{C}}_{6}{\hat{C}}_{2}\right)\right]\sum_{n}W_{2n+1}\ ,

where
\epsilon=\pm 1
reflects the position of the T-dual D6-branes on top of the
\text{O}8_{-}
–plane or on the
\text{O}8_{+}
–plane, respectively. There are no physical couplings to the R-R sector, and therefore these branes are uncharged. The loop-channel open amplitudes are instead equal to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{77}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\eta^{8}}\left[N{\overline{N}}\left(V_{8}-S_{8}\right)+\frac{N^{2}+\overline{N}^{2}}{2}\left(O_{8}-C_{8}\right)\right]\sum_{m}P_{m}\ ,

(D.1.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{7}=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{2\epsilon}{\hat{\eta}^{5}\hat{\vartheta}_{2}}\right)\left[\frac{N+{\overline{N}}}{2}\left({\hat{O}}_{6}{\hat{O}}_{2}+{\hat{V}}_{6}{\hat{V}}_{2}\right)+\frac{N-{\overline{N}}}{2}\left({\hat{S}}_{6}{\hat{C}}_{2}-{\hat{C}}_{6}{\hat{S}}_{2}\right)\right]\sum_{m}(-1)^{m}P_{m}\ .

The gauge group of these branes is
\mathrm{U}(N)
, associated with a complete
{\cal N}=4
super Yang–Mills massless multiplet. In addition, there are complex tachyons in the symmetric plus antisymmetric representations of
\mathrm{U}(N)
, so that, for
\epsilon=1
, the tachyon disappears from a single
N=1
D7-brane at radius
R\leq\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}
.
1
1

1

Note that this dynamics is different with respect to what happens in the type I string, where the existemce of background D9-branes – absent in the Dabholkar–Park orientifold under consideration – implies the presence of tachyons in the D7–D9 sector.

However, the transverse Möbius amplitude (
D.1.1
) gives, in this setup, a positive contribution to the potential energy. This is interpreted, in the T-dual picture, as the attraction of the D6-brane from the
\text{O}8_{-}
–plane, where it is stable, towards the O
8_{+}
–plane, where instead it develops a tachyon
[
285
]
. This process is indeed allowed, given that the appropriate brane positions moduli on the circle do exist. This implies that no D7-brane wrapping the circle can be stable.

D.1.2
Non-BPS D3-branes wrapping the circle

The non-BPS D3-branes wrapping the circle are uncharged and their behavior exactly matches that of the wrapping D7-branes discussed above. The transverse channel amplitudes are equal to

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}_{33}=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-2}v}{2}\frac{1}{\eta^{8}}\left[\left(N+{\overline{N}}\right)^{2}V_{8}+\left(N-{\overline{N}}\right)^{2}S_{8}\right]\sum_{n}W_{n}\ ,

(D.1.3)

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{3}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{8\epsilon v\hat{\eta}}{\hat{\vartheta}_{2}^{3}}\right)\left[\left(N+{\overline{N}}\right)\left({\hat{O}}_{2}{\hat{V}}_{6}-{\hat{V}}_{2}{\hat{O}}_{6}\right)-\left(N-{\overline{N}}\right)\left({\hat{S}}_{2}{\hat{S}}_{6}-{\hat{C}}_{2}{\hat{C}}_{6}\right)\right]\sum_{n}W_{2n+1}\ ,

where
\epsilon=\pm 1
signals that the branes sit on top of the O
8\mp
–plane. The loop-channel open amplitudes are

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{33}=

\displaystyle\left[N{\overline{N}}\left(\frac{V_{8}-S_{8}}{\eta^{8}}\right)+\frac{N^{2}+\overline{N}^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{O_{8}-C_{8}}{\eta^{8}}\right)\right]\sum_{m}P_{m}\ ,

(D.1.4)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{3}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{8\epsilon\hat{\eta}}{\hat{\vartheta}_{2}^{3}}\right)\left[\frac{N+{\overline{N}}}{2}\left({\hat{O}}_{2}{\hat{O}}_{6}+{\hat{V}}_{2}{\hat{V}}_{6}\right)+\frac{N-{\overline{N}}}{2}\left({\hat{S}}_{2}{\hat{C}}_{6}-{\hat{C}}_{2}{\hat{S}}_{6}\right)\right]\sum_{m}(-1)^{m}P_{m}\ .

The gauge group is
\mathrm{U}(N)
and there is a complete
{\cal N}=4
super Yang–Mills multiplet at the massless level. There are complex scalar tachyons in all configurations but that with a single
N=1
D3-brane and
\epsilon=-1
, where the tachyon disappears at small radius
R<\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}
. On the other hand, the associated transverse Möbius amplitude (
D.1.3
) gives a positive contribution to the potential energy, so that the
\epsilon=1
setup is energetically favored, but the corresponding D3-brane has a symmetric
\bm{\frac{N(N+1)}{2}}
tachyon for all
N
.

D.1.3
Even-dimensional branes

Except for the D4 and D8-branes wrapping the circle – which are related to the orthogonal D3 and D7-branes through a kink transition
[
294
]
, as we discuss in Section
3.3
– all the other even D
p
brane are found to be unstable. We begin with those wrapping the circle. The transverse channel amplitudes are given by

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}_{pp}=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{-\frac{p+1}{2}}v}{2}\frac{N^{2}}{\eta^{8}}\left(V_{p-1}O_{9-p}+O_{p-1}V_{9-p}\right)\sum_{n}W_{n}\ ,

(D.1.5)

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{p}=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{2}vN\epsilon}{\hat{\eta}^{p-1}}\left(\frac{2\hat{\eta}}{\hat{\vartheta}_{2}}\right)^{\frac{9-p}{2}}\left({\hat{V}}_{p-1}{\hat{O}}_{9-p}-{\hat{O}}_{p-1}{\hat{V}}_{9-p}\right)\sum_{n}W_{2n+1}\ ,

where
\epsilon=\pm 1
identifies the position of the T-dual D(
p-1
)-brane on either the
\text{O}8_{-}
or to the
\text{O}8_{+}
–plane. As for the D8-brane discussed in Section
3.3
, there are neither physical nor unphysical couplings to R-R fields. The former are not allowed by construction, whereas the presence of the latter would be related to background magnetic fields, which will turn them into physical couplings to R-R forms that, however, do not exist for the even D
p
branes under consideration.

The loop-channel open amplitudes are equal to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{pp}

\displaystyle=\frac{N^{2}}{2}\frac{1}{\eta^{8}}\left[(O_{p-1}+V_{p-1})(O_{9-p}+V_{9-p})-2S^{\prime}_{p-1}S^{\prime}_{9-p}\right]\sum_{m}P_{m}\ ,

(D.1.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{p}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\epsilon N}{\sqrt{2}}\ \frac{1}{\hat{\eta}^{p-1}}\left(\frac{2\hat{\eta}}{\hat{\vartheta}_{2}}\right)^{\frac{9-p}{2}}\left[\sin\tfrac{(p-5)\pi}{4}\left({\hat{O}}_{p-1}{\hat{O}}_{9-p}+{\hat{V}}_{p-1}{\hat{V}}_{9-p}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 119.50157pt+\cos\tfrac{(p-5)\pi}{4}\left({\hat{O}}_{p-1}{\hat{V}}_{9-p}-{\hat{V}}_{p-1}{\hat{O}}_{9-p}\right)\right]\sum_{m}(-1)^{m}P_{m}\;.

The gauge group is either
\mathrm{SO}(N)
or
\mathrm{USp}(N)
, and there is a tachyon in either the symmetric or the antisymmetric representation. The massless tachyon can be removed in the
\mathrm{SO}(1)
configuration if in the antisymmetric representation, which is the case for
p=0
mod 4 and
\epsilon=(-1)^{\frac{p}{4}}
. This confirms that D4 and D8 are the only stable even branes wrapping the circle.

Let us now turn to the even D-branes orthogonal to the circle. The cylinder amplitudes are given by

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}_{pp}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{-\frac{p+1}{2}}}{2v\ \eta^{8}}\left(e^{\frac{i\pi m}{2}}N+e^{-\frac{i\pi m}{2}}{\overline{N}}\right)^{2}\left(V_{p-1}O_{9-p}+O_{p-1}V_{9-p}\right)\sum_{m}P_{m}\ ,

(D.1.7)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{pp}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{8}}\left(N{\overline{N}}\sum_{n}W_{n}+\frac{N^{2}+{\overline{N}}^{2}}{2}\sum_{n}W_{n+\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left[(O_{p-1}+V_{p-1})(O_{9-p}+V_{9-p})-2S^{\prime}_{p-1}S^{\prime}_{9-p}\right]\ .

There is no Möbius amplitude, as the Klein bottle (
3.3.18
) only involves non-vanishing winding numbers. The gauge group is
\mathrm{U}(N)
, as signaled by the complex Chan-Paton factors. Tachyons are present for any value of the radius, and they transform in the adjoint representation of
\mathrm{U}(N)
. There exist also charged scalars, which are tachyonic for
R<\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}
. Hence, orthogonal, even-dimensional branes are unstable for any value of the radius.

D.2
Additional branes of the new Scherk–Schwarz orientifold

The D-branes of the new Scherk–Schwarz orientifold (
3.4.1
) constructed in
[
3
]
that we report here are the non-BPS D5-branes wrapping the circle, the charged D3-branes and the non-BPS D1-branes orthogonal to the circle, along with all even-dimensional branes. The wrapping D5-branes resembles the behavior of the D9-branes, whereas the charged orthogonal D3-branes are similar to the orthogonal D7-branes and the orthogonal D1-branes are similar to the orthogonal D5-branes. These analogue branes are discussed in Section
3.4.3
. Instead, the even-dimensional branes behave like those of the Dabholkar–Park orientifold, discussed in Section
3.3.3
and in Appendix
D.1
.

D.2.1
Non-BPS D5-branes wrapping the circle

The wrapping D5-branes are uncharged and are parameterized as the D9-branes discussed in Section
3.4.3
. In the case of a single type of branes, the cylinder and Möbius amplitudes in the transverse channel are

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}_{55}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{-3}v}{2\eta^{8}}\sum_{n}\left[(N+{\overline{N}})^{2}(V_{8}W_{2n}+O_{8}W_{2n+1})+(N-{\overline{N}})^{2}(S_{8}W_{2n}+C_{8}W_{2n+1})\right]\ ,

(D.2.1)

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{5}

\displaystyle=-\left(\frac{4v}{\hat{\eta}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{2}^{2}}\right)\sum_{n}\left[\epsilon_{1}(N+{\overline{N}})\left({\hat{O}}_{4}{\hat{O}}_{4}-{\hat{V}}_{4}{\hat{V}}_{4}\right)+i(-1)^{n}\epsilon_{2}(N-{\overline{N}})\left({\hat{S}}_{4}{\hat{C}}_{4}-{\hat{C}}_{4}{\hat{S}}_{4}\right)\right]W_{2n+1}\ .

The direct channel amplitudes are instead

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{55}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{8}}\left[N\overline{N}\sum_{m}\left(V_{8}P_{m}-S_{8}P_{m+1/2}\right)+\frac{N^{2}+\overline{N}^{2}}{2}\sum_{m}\left(O_{8}P_{m}-C_{8}P_{m+1/2}\right)\right]\ ,

(D.2.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{5}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2}{\hat{\eta}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{2}^{2}}\right)\sum_{m}(-1)^{m}\left[\epsilon_{1}(N+\overline{N})\left({\hat{O}}_{4}{\hat{O}}_{4}-{\hat{V}}_{4}{\hat{V}}_{4}\right)P_{m}+\epsilon_{2}(N-\overline{N})\left({\hat{S}}_{4}{\hat{C}}_{4}-{\hat{C}}_{4}{\hat{S}}_{4}\right)P_{m+1/2}\right]\ .

The gauge group is
\mathrm{U}(N)
and the massless spectrum contains, in addition to the gauge vectors, four adjoint scalars and complex tachyonic scalars in the symmetric/antisymmetric representation for
\epsilon_{1}=\pm 1
, respectively. These tachyons can be antisymmetrized for
\epsilon_{1}=-1
, but even in this case the first Kaluza–Klein mode of the scalar is still tachyonic for
R>\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}
. Hence, there is no value of the radius for which both the closed string and the D5-brane tachyons are eliminated, except for
R=\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}
where both scalars are massless. At large radius the closed string tachyon is massive, but the D5-brane is unstable.

The instability of the D5-brane has the following interpretation in F-theory. Consider the theory on one extra circle of coordinate
X^{8}
and a D5-brane wrapping both
X^{8}
and
X^{9}
. After T-duality along this circle, the D5-brane is mapped into an M5-brane wrapped over the M-theory circle:

{\rm D5}_{012389}\underset{{\rm T-duality}}{\rightarrow}{\rm M5}_{012391\hskip-0.85358pt0}\;.

(D.2.3)

However, there is no 2-cycle in
\mathcal{M}_{3}
along
(X^{9},X^{10})
, which suggests that the brane is unstable. Similar conclusions are reached considering a D5-brane orthogonal to
X^{8}
, with

{\rm D5}_{012349}\underset{{\rm T-duality}}{\rightarrow}{\rm KK6}_{0123489}\;,

(D.2.4)

since the there is no 2-cycle in
\mathcal{M}_{3}
along
(X^{8},X^{9})
.

D.2.2
Charged D3-branes orthogonal to the circle

These D3-branes are charged and follow the same behavior as the charged D7-branes orthogonal to the circle discussed in Section
3.4.3
. The loop and transverse cylinder amplitudes are equal to

\displaystyle A_{33}=\sum_{n}\left(N\overline{N}W_{n}+\frac{N^{2}+\overline{N}^{2}}{2}W_{n+1/2}\right)\frac{V_{8}-S_{8}}{\eta^{8}}\;,

(D.2.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}_{33}&=\frac{2^{-2}}{2v}\left(\frac{V_{8}-S_{8}}{\eta^{8}}\right)\sum_{m}\left[e^{\frac{i\pi m}{2}}N+e^{-\frac{i\pi m}{2}}\overline{N}\right]^{2}P_{m}\\
&=\frac{2^{-2}}{v}\left(\frac{V_{8}-S_{8}}{\eta^{8}}\right)\sum_{m}\left[N\overline{N}+(-1)^{m}\frac{N^{2}+\overline{N}^{2}}{2}\right]P_{m}\;.\end{aligned}

(D.2.6)

The transverse amplitude tells that there is no closed-string state that can be exchanged between the D3-brane and the O9-plane, since the associated Klein bottle transverse amplitude of eq. (
3.4.4
) only involves massive winding states. The Möbius amplitude is therefore absent, and the cylinder one alone describes the dynamics of the brane even in the presence of the orientifold. The theory living on the worldvolume of the orthogonal D3-brane is found to be
{\cal N}=4
super-Yang–Mills. It is known that this theory has a strong-weak coupling S-duality, thus supporting the conjectured S-duality of the full non-supersymmetric orientifold construction (
3.4.1
).

From the F-theory perspective, this orthogonal charged D3-brane is mapped by T-duality to

\displaystyle{\rm D3}_{0128}\underset{{\rm T-duality}}{\rightarrow}{\rm M2}_{012}\ ,

\displaystyle{\rm D3}_{0123}\underset{{\rm T-duality}}{\rightarrow}{\rm M5}_{012381\hskip-0.85358pt0}\ ,

(D.2.7)

where the M2-brane is point-like in
\mathcal{M}_{3}
, while the M5-brane wraps the
\mathbb{Z}
2-cycle in
\mathcal{M}_{3}
.

D.2.3
Non-BPS D1-branes orthogonal to the circle

D1-branes orthogonal to the circle resemble the behavior of the orthogonal D5-branes discussed in Section
3.4.3
. A single D1-brane is stable at large radius

R\geq\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}
, while multiple D1-branes annihilate each other.

In the F-theory picture, the corresponding T-dual branes would be

\displaystyle{\rm D1}_{01}\underset{{\rm T-duality}}{\rightarrow}{\rm M2}_{018}\;,

\displaystyle{\rm D1}_{08}\underset{{\rm T-duality}}{\rightarrow}{\rm KK}(X^{1\hskip-0.85358pt0})\ ,

(D.2.8)

where the M2-brane wraps the torsion
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
1-cycle along
X^{8}
in
\mathcal{M}_{3}
, while the KK-brane is along the circle
X^{10}
that is only conserved modulo two. In both cases one obtains that a single D1 brane orthogonal to the circle naturally carries a conserved
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
charge, associated with its stability.

D.2.4
Even-dimensional branes

Finally, we describe the even-dimensional D
p
-branes,
i.e.
with
p=0,2,4,6,8
. These branes come in two different configurations, related to the sign of their couplings to the closed-string tachyonic-like scalar.

Let us start with the branes wrapping the circle. The transverse channel open string amplitudes are found to be

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}_{pp}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{-\frac{p+1}{2}}v}{2\eta^{8}}\left[(N_{1}{+}N_{2})^{2}V_{8}\sum_{n}W_{2n}+(N_{1}{-}N_{2})^{2}O_{8}\sum_{n}W_{2n+1}\right]\ ,

(D.2.9)

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mathcal{M}}}_{p}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{2}\ v\ (N_{1}{-}N_{2})\ \frac{1}{\hat{\eta}^{p-1}}\left(\frac{2\hat{\eta}}{\hat{\vartheta}_{2}}\right)^{\frac{9-p}{2}}\left({\hat{O}}_{p-1}{\hat{O}}_{9-p}+{\hat{V}}_{p-1}{\hat{V}}_{9-p}\right)\sum_{n}W_{2n+1}\ ,

where the usual sign
\epsilon=\pm 1
can be absorbed in the definition of
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
. Similarly to the supersymmetric Dabholkar–Park orientifold (see Appendix
3.3.3
), there are neither physical nor unphysical couplings to the R-R sector. The loop-channel open amplitudes are then

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{pp}

\displaystyle=\frac{N_{1}^{2}+N_{2}^{2}}{2\eta^{8}}\left[(O_{p-1}+V_{p-1})(O_{9-p}+V_{9-p})\sum_{m}P_{m}-2S^{\prime}_{p-1}S^{\prime}_{9-p}\sum_{m}P_{m+\frac{1}{2}}\right]

(D.2.10)

\displaystyle+\frac{N_{1}N_{2}}{\eta^{8}}\left[(O_{p-1}+V_{p-1})(O_{9-p}+V_{9-p})\sum_{m}P_{m+\frac{1}{2}}-2S^{\prime}_{p-1}S^{\prime}_{9-p}\sum_{m}P_{m}\right]\ ,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{p}

\displaystyle=-\ \frac{N_{1}-N_{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\ \frac{1}{\hat{\eta}^{p-1}}\left(\frac{2\hat{\eta}}{\hat{\vartheta}_{2}}\right)^{\frac{9-p}{2}}\left[\sin\tfrac{(p-5)\pi}{4}\left({\hat{V}}_{p-1}{\hat{O}}_{9-p}-{\hat{O}}_{p-1}{\hat{V}}_{9-p}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 113.81102pt+\cos\tfrac{(p-5)\pi}{4}\left({\hat{O}}_{p-1}{\hat{O}}_{9-p}+{\hat{V}}_{p-1}{\hat{V}}_{9-p}\right)\right]\sum_{m}(-1)^{m}P_{m}\ .

The gauge group is either
\mathrm{SO}
or
\mathrm{USp}
and the spectrum contains brane-position moduli, Dirac fermions in the symmetric and the antisymmetric representation, and potentially tachyonic real scalars, also in the antisymmetric or the symmetric representation of the gauge group. The tachyons are removed for a single D6-brane with
(N_{1},N_{2})=(1,0)
and for a single D2-brane with
(N_{1},N_{2})=(0,1)
. The gauge group is
\mathrm{SO}(1)=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
in both cases, and the corresponding branes have no Wilson-line moduli (T-dual to brane positions) on the circle. However, these open-string tachyons disappear in the small radius regime
R\leq\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}
, where the closed-string scalar is instead tachyonic. Thus, there are no stable even-dimensional D
p
-brane wrapping the circle in the perturbative regime. In particular, in the large radius limit
R>\sqrt{2\alpha^{\prime}}
the unstable D6 and D2-branes are expected to decay, respectively, into a stable D5 and D1-brane orthogonal to the circle, following the tachyonic kink transition discussed in
[
294
]
. This dynamics takes place also for the D8 and D4-branes in the Dabholkar–Park orientifold, as described in Section
3.3.3
and Appendix
D.1
.

Finally, the behavior of the even branes orthogonal to the circle matches that of the supersymmetric Dabholkar–Park case, and are indeed unstable for all values of the radius.
